This boundary writes no journal signal, applied record, terminal, effect completion, cap draw, box message, ticket, configuration key, or constant. Successful return supplies completed stop evidence to activation; the later activation alone records application after executor unwind. Existing run terminal and restart-reconciliation writers retain custody and record shapes.
- **Observable:** Explicitly invoke the dormant boundary with the real predecessor abort operation and owned worker tasks. Await executor abort and observation to completion BEFORE issuing any worker cancellation, including cancellation of the worker owning the active dispatch. A barrier-held executor unwind must leave every worker uncancelled. If abort fails, propagate that failure without cancelling workers or claiming completed kill. After successful executor unwind, cancel only the supplied live workers and await and retrieve every supplied worker's outcome, including already-completed tasks, cancellation, and exceptions raised during cleanup. Return only when all owned workers have finished; no pending task or unobserved exception may remain. An empty or already-finished worker set still observes executor unwind and is safe to stop.

  Repeated or concurrent stops for the same owned task set share one protected stop operation; they must not re-cancel workers during cleanup or affect a later task set. Repeated cancellation of a stop waiter cannot interrupt executor unwind, issue worker cancellation early, or abandon worker cleanup: finish the protected stop before propagating waiter cancellation. Do not convert a killed invocation into successful ticket output, fabricate a completion or terminal, wipe a restart-reconcilable run, or perform failure routing here. Observing worker outcomes is required cleanup, not the later failure-suppression policy. Without matching kill authorization, leave workers and the executor untouched and preserve ordinary task ownership and failure behavior.

  Construction is behaviorally dormant under `19.I`: real CLI `run`/`drain` and the production-composition harness neither bind nor invoke worker stop. Explicit test wiring proves this boundary only; `chupa.daemon` is already reachable, so import absence is not evidence. `kill-cli-activation` leaves worker stop dormant. `serve-activation` composes the real worker tasks, activates this boundary with decision-before-mutation and executor-unwind-before-worker-cancel ordering, and migrates its dormancy assertion.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_kill_worker_stop.py` adds `test_executor_unwinds_before_worker_cancel` (matching durable kill decision, real predecessor abort, barrier-held executor cleanup, zero worker cancellations before unwind), `test_worker_stop_requires_matching_kill` (no kill, pause/resume, undecided and stale/rejected requests, other lifecycle; zero abort or worker mutation), `test_worker_stop_observes_all_workers` (live, completed, cancelled, and exceptional workers, empty set, barrier-held worker cleanup, every outcome retrieved and no leaked task), `test_worker_stop_is_atomic_under_repeated_cancellation` (concurrent/repeated stops and repeatedly cancelled waiters, one protected stop, no second cancellation during cleanup, cancellation propagates only after cleanup, later tasks unaffected), `test_executor_abort_failure_does_not_cancel_workers` (original abort error propagated, workers untouched, no fabricated journal records or worktree wipe), and `test_kill_worker_stop_is_dormant` (calibrated raising stop probe over real CLI run/drain and the production graph; deliberate wiring trips it, ordinary composition leaves it untouched). Verify with `uv run pytest tests/test_kill_worker_stop.py tests/test_kill_executor_abort.py tests/test_daemon_tasks.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py`; unchanged predecessor suites are preservation suites. Use injected seams, disposable directories, and asyncio barriers, never real-model calls or wall-clock sleeps. Cancelling workers before executor unwind, returning before worker cleanup, or stopping an unmatched lifecycle must fail its named test.

### 19.P3.kill-cli-activation Live bootstrap drain kill

- **Owner:** `chupa/__main__.py` owns the additive `kill` verb and its routing through the existing `_control` lock/inbox path, before pipeline or lifecycle construction. `chupa/daemon.py` owns applying the current lifecycle's accepted kill through the shared `PauseConsumer`, and forwarding executor abort through the existing callable `TicketWriter`. `chupa/stages.py` exposes only `StageContext.abort_current()`, an async forwarding operation to that context's existing `Driver.abort_current()` from `kill-executor-abort`; `TicketWriter.abort_current()` awaits that operation. Neither constructs another Driver, reaches into Driver's private futures, nor implements a second abort path. The merged `runner.bind` already returns `TicketWriter` over the production `StageContext`; the callable `Pipeline`/`Dispatch` composition carries this same writer to `drain`, not a separately constructed test executor. `chupa/drain.py` owns concurrent control consumption, the stopping check before offer accounting, and observation of its single in-flight dispatch before releasing the writer lock. No new module is introduced. Preserve the existing composition and callable signatures; any criteria-forced caller or dormancy-test migration is fenced under `19.L`, never silently worked around.
- **Records:** Reuse `<state_dir>/control/inbox/<request_id>.json` and its closed object `{request_id: str, lifecycle_id: str, verb: pause | resume | kill, hold_id: str | null}`. CLI kill supplies a fresh nonempty ASCII letters/digits/underscore/hyphen request id matching the filename, the discovered current lifecycle, `verb: kill`, and null `hold_id`. The existing durable no-overwrite publication seam is the only publisher; duplicate destination requires a new request id. Reuse discovery `<state_dir>/control/active.json`, `{lifecycle_id: nonempty str, hold_id: nonempty str | null}` while live and literal JSON null on retirement. It is a projection, never authority to retarget a stale request or create a lifecycle.

  Reuse `CONTROL_DECISION = "control_decision"`, owned by `chupa/control.py`, written only by the lock-holding inbox as `EventType.SIGNAL`, null ticket/key, body `{kind: control_decision, request_id: str, lifecycle_id: str | null, verb: pause | resume | kill | null, hold_id: str | null, decision: accepted | stale | rejected, reason: str}`. Valid current-lifecycle kill is accepted; valid prior-lifecycle kill is stale and mutates nothing; malformed requests are rejected with the predecessor's shape road and safe identities. Decision append/fsync precedes the stopping latch and every abort or cancellation. The existing request-id fold remains the exactly-once authority. Read `ControlProjection.lifecycle_id` and latched `kill_requested`; pause/resume never clear stopping. An undecided file, stale/rejected decision, or another lifecycle's projection cannot stop dispatch.

  `chupa/daemon.py` owns `KILL_APPLIED = "kill_applied"`. Its lock-holding application boundary is the sole writer of `EventType.SIGNAL`, null ticket/key, closed body `{kind: kill_applied, request_id: nonempty str, lifecycle_id: nonempty str}`. Write one application signal per accepted current-lifecycle kill request, only after successful executor abort and observation of the drain's in-flight dispatch cleanup. Its identities must match that accepted kill decision. Repeated scans and reconstruction read application records by request id and never duplicate them; multiple accepted kill requests share one stop/unwind, each getting its own completion evidence afterwards. Failed decision append invokes no mutation; failed abort/observation or failed application append never reports applied success. Acceptance without application remains visibly incomplete. A fresh lifecycle ignores both prior-lifecycle acceptance and application, and ordinary on-entry reconciliation handles any orphan left by the interrupted lifecycle.

  Stopping and active dispatch/cleanup references are memory-only ownership, recovered from the existing decision projection where applicable. `chupa/drain.py` adds memory-only `Report.killed: bool = False`: a completed kill sets it, leaves `handoff` null, returns the existing non-quiescent `EXIT_TICKET = 1`, and renders a kill stop with the continuing `uv run python -m chupa drain` command. Preserve the runtime-ceiling `halted` field, `HALT_SIGNAL = "drain_halted"`, its `{signal: drain_halted, ceiling: drain.max_runtime_hours, hours: number, merged: list[str]}` body, and ordinary quiescence exit 0; kill is not a ceiling trip. Live CLI submission exits 0 with `kill submitted: <request_id>`; idle kill refuses with `nothing running to kill` and `EXIT_REFUSED = 2`, with the road to start a drain before submitting kill. Allocate no lifecycle, request, decision, applied signal, or latch on the idle path. No new event type, terminal state, Effect key, cap, ticket frontmatter, configuration field, notification, or processed marker is introduced. Existing run-state transitions, LLM intent/completion keys, harvest, terminal, and restart-reconciliation writers keep their custody and shapes.
- **Observable:** Against a live bootstrap drain, CLI kill takes the existing lock-contention route: read discovery and publish only the bound request, never append to the journal or call the pipeline in the second CLI. Missing, malformed, retired, or replaced discovery refuses with the road to read the running engine's current control identity and submit a new request. A lifecycle change between discovery and consumption leaves the original request stale; never silently retarget it. With no running engine, take and release the lock solely to refuse kill, even if stale discovery remains. Preserve live and idle pause/resume behavior.

  The lock-holding drain consumes its ONE shared inbox serially at offer boundaries and concurrently with an in-flight dispatch, using its injected sleep seam for wakeup; kill must not wait for the model call or a pause to finish. Construction starts no control task or external work. On accepted current-lifecycle kill, latch stopping before execution changes, await the real composed writer's executor-abort boundary, then cancel if needed and await the drain's dispatch continuation and retrieve its outcome. Protect this cleanup from repeated cancellation and concurrent/repeated kill; executor unwind must finish before dispatch cancellation, application recording, discovery retirement, or lock release. This is ownership of the drain's single dispatch continuation, not activation of `DaemonTasks` worker stop. Idle executor abort is safe when no Driver invocation is active; kill during stage preparation, Verification, or between calls still observes dispatch cleanup and cannot start a later call. Abort/observation failure propagates, without a false application signal or successful kill report.

  Once stopping is latched, no fresh ticket, retry re-offer, automatic keep, config snapshot, running transition, or retry-cap draw is admitted. Check before ALL offer accounting, including after a preparation or pause wait, and before any self-upgrade handoff; an accepted kill cannot launch a child drain. A paused or otherwise held drain remains able to accept kill without resume. Preserve normal selection, rescan, retry, free premise/spec-gap re-entry, pause, and ceiling behavior when no matching kill exists. Cancellation from deliberate kill is not a timeout, infra_error, successful stage result, or permission to re-prompt. A terminal already durably written by normal completion remains authoritative. Otherwise leave the killed run's `running` transition and incomplete intents restart-reconcilable, with its worktree retained for ordinary on-entry harvest -> abandoned terminal -> removal; never fabricate a completion or terminal, erase history, or wipe an open run merely to make kill green. Await all owned control and dispatch cleanup, retire discovery through the filesystem seam before releasing the lock, and leave no task or unobserved exception behind.

  Migrate only `test_kill_signal_journal_is_dormant` and `test_executor_abort_is_dormant` in the two fenced predecessor suites to positive live-drain activation assertions, retaining their component ordering, identity, atomicity, and ordinary timeout tests. `kill-worker-stop` and `kill-failure-suppression` remain behaviorally dormant through real CLI run/drain and the production-composition harness; their unchanged suites are preservation suites. Only `serve-activation` composes their real worker tasks and migrates those dormancy assertions. This row adds neither `serve` nor a test-only worker graph as production evidence.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_kill_cli_activation.py` names `test_live_kill_publishes_only_bound_request` (real CLI, held lock, exact durable request, submission output/exit, no second journal writer, consumer, lifecycle, or pipeline); `test_idle_kill_refuses_without_state` (idle lock acquisition/release, exit 2 and exact reason, absent/stale discovery, no request or governed record); and `test_kill_discovery_and_lifecycle_races_fail_closed` (missing/malformed/retired identity, lock race, stale-lifecycle decision with zero abort, no retargeting, pause/resume preserved). `test_live_drain_kill_reaches_production_driver` drives real CLI drain -> production Pipeline/Runner -> TicketWriter -> StageContext -> Driver with a cancellation-resistant scripted LLM and barrier-held unwind, asserting one shared inbox, durable decision before mutation, abort before cancellation, no further model call, and application after dispatch cleanup. A disconnected forwarding operation or prematurely written application signal must fail it.

  `test_kill_stops_before_all_offer_accounting` covers fresh dispatch, retry and automatic-keep draws, free premise/spec-gap re-entry, pause/preparation waits, kill while no Driver call is active, and a pending self-upgrade: no post-acceptance snapshot, running event, cap draw, next call, or child launch. `test_kill_application_is_once_and_identity_bound` covers retained/repeated request files, multiple accepted kills sharing cleanup, reconstruction at acceptance/application crash points, exact completion bodies, append failures, and a fresh lifecycle ignoring old records. `test_kill_cleanup_is_atomic_and_failures_propagate` covers repeatedly cancelled waiters, completion races, executor failure, observed exceptions, discovery retirement before lock release, no premature application or orphan task, and truthful report/exit. `test_killed_run_is_terminal_or_restart_reconcilable` checks the producing journal with the invariant auditor, preserves already-written terminals, proves incomplete effects are not fabricated, and drives ordinary next-invocation reconciliation to harvest and abandon the retained orphan before fresh dispatch. `test_kill_activation_keeps_worker_boundaries_dormant` uses calibrated raising probes on the real run/drain composition to prove no worker-stop or failure-suppression call; deliberately wired probes must trip. Predecessor tests become `test_kill_signal_journal_is_active_in_drain` and `test_executor_abort_is_active_in_drain`, exercising the same production path rather than explicitly wiring a second executor. Use disposable repositories, injected clock/sleep/process/filesystem seams, scripted LLMs, and asyncio barriers, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits. Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_kill_cli_activation.py tests/test_kill_signal_journal.py tests/test_kill_executor_abort.py tests/test_kill_worker_stop.py tests/test_kill_failure_suppression.py tests/test_control.py tests/test_control_cli.py tests/test_daemon_tasks.py tests/test_driver.py tests/test_stages.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_reconcile.py`; unchanged suites are preservation suites, and every criteria-forced migration is fenced under `19.L`.

### 19.P3.heartbeat Dormant daemon heartbeat and external freshness check

- **Owner:** `chupa/heartbeat.py` owns the heartbeat file writer and the deterministic external freshness check. `chupa/daemon.py` owns a dormant, explicitly invoked cycle boundary that supplies the watched core's health to that writer. Reuse the existing `FileSystem.write` and injected `Clock`; filesystem metadata reads are supplied as an injected callback, not performed raw in engine code. Preserve `DaemonTasks`, `DaemonAdmission`, and the production composition's existing signatures and ownership. Continuous-loop wiring belongs to `serve-activation`, not this construction.
- **Records:** The sole heartbeat writer atomically writes empty bytes to `<state_dir>/heartbeat` through `FileSystem.write`, once per explicitly requested healthy main-loop cycle. The path comes from the host's existing `Config.state_dir`; it is instance-local, uncommitted, and contains no journal record, run identity, secret, or progress data. Its modification time is the liveness evidence; do not add a journal `checkpoint`, signal, effect key, frontmatter value, config field, or counter. The dormant daemon boundary takes four required named zero-argument boolean health callbacks: `workers`, `merge_queue`, `box_consumer`, and `watcher`. Each callback is supplied by the task owner and reports whether that core component is live and responsive for this cycle; a running ticket or an idle consumer can be healthy. Task existence or `not task.done()` alone is insufficient evidence of responsiveness. The boundary evaluates all four before writing and returns `True` only after a successful write; any false result returns `False` without touching the file. A callback or write exception propagates, never becomes a healthy result.

  The external check takes the same explicit heartbeat path, an injected metadata reader returning its timezone-aware modification time as `datetime | None` (`None` means missing), an injected `Clock`, and a strictly positive `timedelta` maximum age selected by the external checker caller. It returns `True` exactly when the file exists and `0 <= clock() - mtime <= max_age`; missing, stale, or future-dated evidence returns `False`. Refuse a nonpositive maximum age or timezone-naive time with a paved road to supply a positive duration and timezone-aware times. Metadata-read failures propagate so the external caller cannot report healthy. No engine polling interval or freshness config key is introduced.
- **Observable:** Construction invokes no health callback, starts no task, and writes no file. An explicit healthy cycle refreshes the file; a dead, cancelled, completed, or unresponsive watched component supplies false health and stops refreshes, allowing the external age check to turn unhealthy. A blocked main loop cannot refresh the file either. The boundary neither restarts nor kills tasks and never treats heartbeat freshness as ticket progress, successful verification, or merge evidence. The external check is a dumb read-only operation usable by an operator's systemd timer or cron integration: no LLM, subprocess launch, journal write, notification transport, service-unit generation, or daemon control mutation. The operator owns scheduling and acting on an unhealthy result.

  Construction is behaviorally dormant under `19.I`: real CLI `run`/`drain` and the production-composition harness neither construct nor invoke the heartbeat cycle boundary and create no heartbeat file. Explicit test wiring exercises it. Do not require `chupa.daemon` import absence, since scheduler activation already makes it reachable. `serve-activation` later owns main-loop invocation with real core-task health and fences `tests/test_heartbeat.py` to migrate the dormancy assertion.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_heartbeat.py` names `test_construction_is_idle` (no callback, task, or file write); `test_healthy_cycle_refreshes_heartbeat` (all four probes evaluated before one empty-byte atomic write, repeated healthy cycles refresh the configured path); `test_unhealthy_core_stops_refresh` (each of the four components independently false, including dead and wedged cases, no write or manufactured healthy result); `test_heartbeat_failures_propagate` (callback and filesystem failures, no false-green return); `test_external_heartbeat_freshness` (injected clock and metadata reader, fresh and exact-age-boundary pass, missing, stale, and future-dated fail, no mutations); `test_external_heartbeat_refuses_invalid_evidence` (nonpositive age, naive times, and metadata-read failure, paved refusals); and `test_heartbeat_is_dormant` (real CLI `run`/`drain` and production graph unchanged, raising construction/cycle probes proven to fail under deliberate wiring, no heartbeat file). Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_heartbeat.py tests/test_daemon_tasks.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py`; all except the new heartbeat test file are unchanged preservation suites. Use injected seams and scripted health/metadata callbacks, never wall-clock waits or real-model calls.

### 19.P3.restart-timers Restart reconciliation, idle orphan sweeping, and durable deadlines

- **Owner:** `chupa/restart.py` owns the lock-held daemon startup coordinator and idle orphan-sweep boundary, delegating orphan detection, harvest, terminal writing, and worktree cleanup to the existing `chupa.reconcile.reconcile`. `chupa/timers.py` owns `Timers`, its journal-derived deadline projection, and timer writes. `chupa/daemon.py` owns the startup-before-admission boundary and supplies active-run/admission ownership to the sweeper; `chupa/__main__.py` composes these through the real `build_daemon_core` root. Reuse the checkout's journal, Git, configured state/worktree roots, `runner.harvest_orphan`, and injected `Clock`/`Sleep`; never construct a second writer or recovery implementation. `serve-activation` later invokes this same startup boundary under its lifetime lock and owns continuous maintenance scheduling; this row adds no `serve` verb or background loop at construction.
- **Records:** Preserve `reconcile.py`'s existing fold: a stem is orphaned when it has a `running` transition or an unmatched effect intent since its last terminal; any terminal clears that run, including non-ok terminals with incomplete effects. The existing reconcile writer harvests an existing orphan worktree with its journal-derived `run_seq`, appends `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION` with body `{to: abandoned}`, the orphan's ticket and null key, then removes its worktree through Git's remove/prune operations. A missing worktree still receives the terminal. Preserve its harvest-error signal `{signal: harvest_failed, error: str}` on the orphan ticket and its subsequent abandonment/cleanup. No completion is fabricated, no history is erased, and a later entry uses the incremented run sequence. `worker-recovery-disposition` owns the later `recovery_alert` addition; this row emits no such signal.

  `Timers` is the sole writer of the already-declared `EventType.TIMER_ARMED` and `EventType.TIMER_FIRED`, at their existing schema version. Both carry the closed body `{timer_id: nonempty str, deadline: str}`, envelope `ticket: str | null` supplied by the timer's owner, and null `key`. `deadline` is an absolute timezone-aware datetime rendered with the journal's `render_ts` convention: UTC `isoformat()` with `+00:00`, never a remaining duration or local/monotonic timestamp. Validate timer bodies at the timer write boundary; reconstruction validates them too. The caller supplies a stable id for ONE deadline and owning ticket. Repeating an identical arm is a no-write replay, including after firing; reusing an id with a different deadline or ticket refuses with the road to use a fresh id for a new deadline. Recurring consumers use fresh ids per occurrence, without cancellation or rescheduling machinery.

  Fold `Journal.read()` in journal order: an armed id without its matching fired record is pending; a matching fired id is completed history and cannot fire again. A fired record retains its arm's exact body and ticket. Malformed bodies, conflicting identities, or a fire without a matching arm fail closed with a road naming the invalid timer record and restoring valid journal evidence before restart. Empty history is a no-op. Pending/fired views are memory-only projections, rebuilt from the journal; no timer file, index, counter, new event type, configuration key, frontmatter, or reserved `checkpoint` write is added. Append/fsync the arm before publishing it as pending, and append/fsync the fire before removing it from pending or returning it to a consumer. An append failure propagates without a successful arm/fire result or consumer action.
- **Observable:** Component construction reads no journal, writes nothing, creates no task, harvests nothing, and dispatches nothing. Explicit daemon startup, while holding the checkout's sole writer lock and before any live run/admission exists, awaits reconciliation, reconstructs the SAME `Timers` lifetime, and fires overdue deadlines before permitting dispatch. The CLI daemon composition awaits this startup through its admission boundary before the first per-dispatch config snapshot or pipeline preparation; concurrent first offers share the startup and cannot dispatch early. Completed startup is not repeated on every offer. Reconciliation, timer-fold, or append failure leaves startup incomplete and dispatch blocked by that failure, never a ready latch set in advance. The existing bootstrap CLI `run`/`drain` lock-held on-entry reconciliation remains intact; the daemon coordinator reuses its machinery rather than changing scaffold terminal or retry behavior. Continuous `serve` later composes this root and startup explicitly, including when its queue is empty.

  Startup re-arms pending deadlines at their original absolute times, without another `timer_armed` event or restart-relative delay. A deadline fires when `clock() >= deadline`, including equality and deadlines already overdue at startup. Process due timers in `(deadline, timer_id)` order. Repeated scans and reconstruction after a durable fire emit nothing for that id. A crash after an arm but before firing leaves it pending on restart; a crash after the fire append leaves it fired. Timer firing records deadline passage, not completion of an external action: consumers read the fired projection on recovery and use the existing Effects seam and key domains for any external side effect. This row builds no retro, checkpoint-push, provider cooldown, or stuck-threshold consumer. An explicitly run deadline wait uses injected `Sleep` against the next clock-derived deadline and rechecks the clock on wake; no wall-clock timeout or constructor-started task. Its owner cancels and awaits it before releasing the writer lock.

  The explicitly invoked orphan sweeper runs only at a serialized idle maintenance boundary under the same writer lock. If the daemon owns any active run, dispatch cleanup, or merge admission, skip the sweep without harvest, terminal, or Git mutation; an open journal run alone is not proof of death while its owner is alive. When idle, use the existing reconcile fold and cleanup order. Repeated sweeps do not abandon a terminal run again. Paths come from the checkout config, never assumed host layout. Git/journal corruption and cleanup failures propagate; startup or sweeping never treats them as successful recovery.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_restart_timers.py` names `test_restart_construction_is_idle` (no journal read, task, callback, or mutation); `test_restart_reuses_orphan_reconciliation` (running and intent-only orphans, harvest -> abandoned -> remove/prune, missing worktree, harvest-error signal, fresh run sequence, terminal-history preservation, repeat no-op); and `test_orphan_sweep_preserves_live_ownership` (each active-run, cleanup, and admission case independently skips all mutations, then idle sweeping reaps the dead run). `test_timer_records_and_identity` pins exact envelopes/bodies, UTC rendering, identical-arm replay before/after fire, conflicting-id refusal, and invalid evidence roads. `test_timer_append_is_write_ahead` injects arm/fire append failures and proves no projection success or consumer action precedes durable publication. `test_timers_rearm_from_journal` reconstructs across segments and restart crash points, preserving absolute future deadlines, firing overdue/equal deadlines once, retaining completed history, and rejecting malformed/unpaired/conflicting records. `test_timer_deadline_wait_uses_injected_sleep` advances a fake clock through early wakeups and ordered simultaneous deadlines with zero wall-clock delay and observes cancellation cleanup.

  `test_production_restart_precedes_dispatch` uses the existing in-process production-composition harness and real CLI `build_daemon_core`, with planted orphan worktrees and persisted pending/fired timers, to prove reconciliation -> re-arm/overdue fire -> snapshot/prepare/dispatch on one shared journal/Timers instance. Concurrent offers cannot cross unfinished startup; a barrier-held reap and injected recovery failures must keep dispatch uncalled. `test_restart_failures_do_not_report_ready` covers corruption, cleanup, fold, and fire failures with no premature startup success. Check the resulting member-local journals with the existing invariant auditor. Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_restart_timers.py tests/test_reconcile.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_daemon_admission.py tests/test_daemon_config.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_audit.py`; unchanged suites are preservation suites. Any criteria-forced caller or predecessor assertion migration is fenced by the seed under `19.L`, rather than silently changing an unfenced test. Use disposable repositories and injected seams, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits.

### 19.P3.flake-detection Dormant flake detection and quarantine

- **Owner:** `chupa/flake.py` owns detection and the journal-derived test quarantine projection. `chupa/daemon.py` owns an explicitly invoked, dormant composition hook supplying the existing lock holder's journal, Box, `Config.caps.quarantine`, and an injected escalation callback. Reuse `Box.enqueue` and the journal's write-ahead append; do not add a second box writer, ledger file, process runner, or notification transport. Construction performs no reads, calls, writes, or task creation. Release policy is the separate `flake-release` row's responsibility.
- **Records:** Detection accepts one nonblank `test_id` identifying the named test and its observed fail-then-pass evidence from a bare re-run in the same workspace without a code change (section 11.5). A whole command's later success without named-test evidence is insufficient. The caller supplies that evidence; this component neither runs a check nor changes its result. File the report through the existing `Box.enqueue(message_class="failure_report", origin=test_id, stage="check", outcome="gate_failed", summary=<nonblank fail-then-pass description>, reason=<failure reason>)`. Both description and reason are supplied by the caller, without secrets. Use the existing Box signature recipe, including its normalization, unchanged: `signature("failure_report", test_id, "check", "gate_failed", reason=reason)`. `origin=test_id` retains exact test identity even when reason normalization removes paths or digits. Box remains the sole writer of its existing message schema; the returned id, whether newly filed or signature-deduped, is `box_id`, and the stored message's signature is `signature`. Add no message class or frontmatter.

  The sole detection writer in `chupa/flake.py` appends `EventType.SIGNAL`, envelope `ticket: null`, `key: flake/<box_id>`, body exactly `{kind: flake_detected, test_id: <nonblank string>, signature: <the Box message's 64-character lowercase SHA-256>, box_id: <the returned Box id>}`. The durable append precedes quarantine visibility. The ledger is a projection over all journal segments, keyed by `box_id`, retaining `test_id`, `signature`, and `box_id`; the blocking-test view is the set of its active `test_id` values, not ticket stems. Existing detection-key replay with identical identity appends nothing and does not re-quarantine a released report; a conflicting identity fails closed with a road to repair the evidence rather than overwrite history. Different reports for an already quarantined test do not consume another simultaneous-test slot.

  The projection also recognizes section 11.5's release record, whose sole writer is `flake-release`: `EventType.SIGNAL`, `ticket: null`, `key: flake-release/<box_id>/<fix_stem>`, body exactly `{kind: flake_released, test_id, signature, box_id, fix_stem}`, all identity strings nonblank. A matching release folds that report out; another active report for the same test keeps the test quarantined. Reject malformed flake records, conflicting detection identities, and releases without a matching detection identity; unrelated journal events are ignored. This construction writes no release and reads no inferred fix stem, ticket frontmatter, or decision-registry record. Later release reads the existing Box `status: resolved` and `resolution: {kind: ticket, link: <fix_stem>}`, not a stem stored in the closed `status` vocabulary (`pending | resolved`).

  Use the existing positive `caps.quarantine` value (shipped default `5`), counting distinct active tests. At the limit, another report for an active test remains admissible; a new test would cross the limit. Still file/dedup its report, but append no detection, leave the existing quarantine unchanged, and invoke the supplied escalation callback for section 13's `quarantine-cap crossed` with a road to fix and release existing quarantines before admitting another. Add no config key, cap-consumption event, escalation record schema, or transport. The callback is supplied explicitly by the dormant hook's caller; callback failures propagate.
- **Observable:** Explicit detection of the named fail-then-pass case files the report and returns the durable quarantine projection; it never calls the flaky run a clean pass. Restart reconstructs the same identities from journal history. A crash after Box publication but before detection append is recoverable by the same signature-deduped call; a crash after append reconstructs the quarantine and replay adds nothing. Box or append failures propagate, with no manufactured successful quarantine. Invalid or insufficient evidence is refused before publication with a road to provide one named test's unchanged-workspace fail-then-pass observation. Cap crossing leaves the new test blocking; it never silently discards another quarantine.

  This is behavioral dormancy under `19.I`: the real CLI `run`/`drain` and existing production-composition harness neither construct nor call the new daemon hook, file a flake report, change verification or merge admission, or filter tests. Direct injected invocation exercises the component. Continuous production wiring belongs to `serve-activation`; that activation must fence and migrate the dormancy assertion under `19.L`. Mechanical release, manual HGATE resume, and actual check execution/filtering are outside this construction.

### 19.P3.flake-release Identity-bound mechanical quarantine release

- **Owner:** `chupa/flake.py` owns mechanical release, extending the detection component and journal-derived quarantine projection of `19.P3.flake-detection`. `chupa/daemon.py` exposes release through the same explicitly invoked, dormant composition boundary, using the existing lock holder's journal and Box. Reuse those owners; add no module, ledger file, box writer, check runner, or notification transport. Construction performs no reads, writes, callbacks, or task creation.
- **Records:** Read the detection identity from the journal: `EventType.SIGNAL`, `ticket: null`, `key: flake/<box_id>`, body exactly `{kind: flake_detected, test_id, signature, box_id}`. Resolve only that `box_id` through `Box.get`; the stored message's id, signature, and `origin` must match the detection's `box_id`, `signature`, and `test_id`, and its class/stage/outcome must be `failure_report`/`check`/`gate_failed`. Box remains the sole writer of the message. The fix identity is the existing message's `status: resolved` and `resolution: {kind: ticket, link: <fix_stem>}`; `status` remains the closed `pending | resolved` vocabulary. Pending messages and tombstone/decision resolutions provide no fix and cannot release. Never infer a stem from a name, summary, frontmatter, or decision-registry record.

  Read the same journal's `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION` with `ticket: <fix_stem>` and body `to: merged`, emitted by the existing merge owner; an `ok` run, another stem's merge, or a proposed fix is insufficient. The caller supplies observed green re-run evidence for the exact quarantined `test_id` against that merged fix in the unchanged workspace/code being checked. A whole command's success without named-test evidence, an unobserved result, evidence from before the fix merged, or another test's pass is insufficient. This component validates evidence but executes no checks and changes no verification result. No new evidence record, merge record, event type, constant, config key, cap draw, or ticket frontmatter is introduced.

  The sole release writer in `chupa/flake.py` appends `EventType.SIGNAL`, envelope `ticket: null`, `key: flake-release/<box_id>/<fix_stem>`, body exactly `{kind: flake_released, test_id, signature, box_id, fix_stem}`. Preserve the detection's identity strings, including its 64-character lowercase SHA-256 signature, and require a nonblank resolved fix stem. Append durably BEFORE folding that report out of quarantine. Reuse the detection unit's validated projection over all journal segments: release must match a detection, and another active report for the same test keeps that test quarantined. An already recorded matching release is a no-op even after restart; it appends nothing and needs no fresh re-run. Conflicting or malformed identities fail closed with a road to repair the source evidence, never overwrite history.
- **Observable:** Explicit release lifts only the report whose Box resolution names the merged fix and whose named test has observed green re-run evidence. A pending/non-ticket resolution, unmerged fix, failed re-run, or absent sufficient evidence leaves quarantine unchanged and writes no release; the road is to resolve the report to its fix ticket, merge that fix, and supply the named test's green re-run. Missing Box messages, identity mismatches, corrupt journal records, and read/append failures propagate without reporting success or changing quarantine. A crash before append leaves the entry active; a crash after durable append reconstructs it released, and replay adds nothing. Releasing one report does not evict another or re-quarantine a previously released detection.

  Release remains behaviorally dormant under `19.I`: real CLI `run`/`drain` and the existing production-composition harness neither construct nor call the flake hook or release/filter quarantined tests. Direct injected invocation exercises release. Continuous production wiring belongs to `serve-activation`, which must fence and migrate the dormancy assertion under `19.L`. Preserve detection, dedup, and quarantine-cap behavior. Manual HGATE `resume` remains section 11.5's control-inbox override, outside this mechanical-release row; do not activate it or add a second release path here.
- **Tests:** Extend `tests/test_flake.py` with `test_release_construction_is_idle` (no reads, writes, callbacks, or tasks); `test_release_resolves_exact_box_identity` (real Box ticket resolution, pending/tombstone/decision no-op, missing message and identity mismatch refusal, no stem inference); `test_release_requires_matching_merge_and_named_green_rerun` (correct merge and observed named pass, another stem, ok-only, unmerged fix, pre-merge evidence, another test, failed/absent/unnamed or changed-code evidence); `test_release_signal_is_write_ahead` (exact envelope/key/body and append before projection change, append failure leaves quarantine active); `test_release_replay_and_restart` (all segments, crash before/after append, repeat no-op without fresh evidence, no duplicate signal); and `test_release_preserves_other_quarantines` (multiple reports for one test, unrelated tests, malformed/unmatched/conflicting release refusal, released detection replay remains released). `test_flake_release_is_dormant` drives the real CLI and production-composition harness with calibrated raising probes, proving deliberate hook wiring trips the assertion and ordinary verification/admission remain unchanged. Keep the detection suite green. Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_flake.py tests/test_box.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py`; unfenced suites run unchanged as preservation suites. Use injected seams and disposable state, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits.

### 19.P3.journal-roll Journal size and age rotation

- **Owner:** The existing `chupa/journal.py` `Journal` owns the roll trigger and segment creation under its supplied state directory, using its injected clock and the checkout's existing single-writer ownership. Add no module, daemon task, timer, or second journal writer; `tests/test_journal_roll.py` owns the new tests.
- **Records:** Define engine constants `ROLL_BYTES = 64 * 1024 * 1024` and `ROLL_AGE = timedelta(hours=24)` in `chupa/journal.py`, never configuration. Read the active segment's current byte length and its first complete event's pinned UTC `ts`; age is injected UTC now minus that timestamp, reconstructed from the segment on restart, never filesystem mtime or a persisted counter. An empty segment has no age and receives its first event without an age roll. Preserve the existing envelope exactly (`v`, `type`, `ts`, `ticket`, `key`, `body`), event versions, closed event vocabulary, and caller-supplied bodies; rotation writes no event, signal, checkpoint, frontmatter, index, or metadata file.

  Before appending a validated event, roll when the existing active segment is at least `ROLL_BYTES` bytes OR its age is at least `ROLL_AGE`. Equality trips; below both thresholds stays on the same segment. A write that crosses the size threshold remains whole in its current segment; the next append rolls. Derive the next sequence from the highest existing sequence, increment by one, and create `<six-digit zero-padded sequence>-<injected UTC YYYYMMDD>.jsonl` under the same journal directory. Same-day rolls have distinct sequences; restart continues existing ordering. Publish the new segment without overwriting an existing path and fsync the directory before reporting its creation successful. `Journal` remains the sole segment writer. Never rename, rewrite, delete, or compact rolled segments.
- **Observable:** Rotation is synchronous within `Journal.append`, requires no production caller or constructor change, and adds no idle polling: a threshold reached while idle rolls on the next append. Construction and reads create no segment. Preserve closed-handle refusal and event validation before mutation, startup truncation of an active torn final line before append or roll, event fsync before append returns, and propagated filesystem/clock/corruption failures without a successful append result. The section 6 live-short-write hazard remains outside this row; add no repair-before-every-roll path.

  `Journal.read_segments()` remains the one parser yielding ordered per-segment event tuples, and `Journal.read()` flattens them unchanged. All prior complete records survive a roll in order, so Effects, timers, caps, and state folds retain their history. Only the newest segment tolerates an unterminated final line; malformed terminated lines and torn tails in older segments still raise `JournalCorruption` with the existing repair road. Rotation must not seal a startup-torn tail into a rolled segment or mask corruption while reading the age anchor. Preserve the existing `Journal(state_dir, clock)` and append/read/close signatures and all direct callers.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_journal_roll.py` names `test_roll_constants_and_size_boundary` (exact constants, below/equal/above threshold, whole crossing event stays put until the next append); `test_roll_age_boundary_uses_injected_clock` (below/equal/above 24 hours, either threshold independently sufficient, first complete event as age anchor, empty segment, UTC dates, no wall-clock wait); `test_roll_sequence_and_restart` (same-day successive rolls, highest existing sequence, restart reconstructs age/size, reads and construction are idle, idle passage rolls only on append); `test_roll_preserves_records_and_read_laws` (old segment bytes unchanged, all envelopes/bodies and event order preserved through both readers, active torn-tail tolerance, rolled and mid-segment corruption refused); `test_startup_tail_repair_precedes_roll` (restart truncates an active torn tail before sealing it, complete records retained); and `test_roll_durability_and_failures` (directory publication and event fsync ordering, no overwrite, creation/write/fsync failures propagate, invalid event and closed handle mutate nothing). Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_journal_roll.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_effects.py tests/test_audit.py`; unchanged suites are preservation suites. Use disposable state and injected clock/filesystem failure probes, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits.

### 19.P3.storm-ledger Dormant storm occurrence ledger

- **Owner:** New `chupa/storm.py` owns the occurrence writer and journal-derived window projection. It receives the existing lock holder's `Journal` and injected clock; `Journal` remains the sole durable writer through its existing append/read seams. `tests/test_storm.py` owns the component and dormancy tests. Add no ledger file, Box writer, daemon hook, task, or production caller. Construction performs no reads, writes, or clock calls.
- **Records:** Each explicitly supplied arrival appends `EventType.SIGNAL`, envelope `ticket: null`, key `storm-occurrence/<signature>/<occurrence_id>`, body exactly `{kind: storm_occurrence, signature, occurrence_id, emitting_stage, emitting_origin}`. `signature` is the existing producer's 64-character lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest, never recomputed by the ledger; `occurrence_id` is a nonblank producer-supplied string unique per arrival and stable on replay. `emitting_stage` and `emitting_origin` are strings or null, retained unchanged, including nulls for producers without a pipeline stage or ticket origin. Do not derive occurrence identity from a Box message id: several arrivals may dedup onto that same message. No Box record, ticket frontmatter, event type, or envelope field changes.

  Validate the occurrence before appending. Fold existing occurrence signals across all segments through `Journal.read()`, using its pinned UTC envelope timestamps and corruption rules. An existing matching key and body is a no-op, even if it has aged out of the live window or is in a rolled segment; it appends nothing and preserves the original timestamp. Reusing a key with different occurrence data, or reading a malformed storm occurrence whose body, key, or null ticket disagree, fails closed without writing; the road is to supply a fresh id for a distinct arrival or repair the producing evidence, never overwrite journal history. Ignore unrelated event kinds. Durably append before exposing the new occurrence in the projection; append/read failures propagate without a successful result.

  Define `STORM_WINDOW = timedelta(hours=1)` in `chupa/storm.py`, the shipped section 12 window, never a new config knob. For a supplied signature, project its occurrences in journal order whose envelope timestamps fall in the half-open interval `(injected UTC now - STORM_WINDOW, injected UTC now]`. The lower boundary is excluded, now is included, and future timestamps are excluded. Count distinct occurrence keys, not Box messages; another signature contributes nothing. Reconstruct from all active and rolled segments on each read, including after restart; store no authoritative counter or cached ledger. Empty history returns no occurrences and count zero; reads create no files or events.
- **Observable:** Direct injected invocation records and counts arrivals, including distinct arrivals with identical signatures; replay records each arrival once. A crash before durable append leaves no recorded occurrence; after durable append, reconstruction and replay retain exactly one with its original timestamp. The ledger does not trip at any count, compute or emit a trip signal, enqueue a message, notify, hold dispatch, or consume resume. The section 12 threshold and deterministic trip identity belong to later trip activation, not this occurrence-only writer. Producer wiring, notification activation, and dispatch hold remain their named registry rows.

  Construction is dormant under `19.I`: `chupa.storm` is absent from the transitive `chupa.*` import closure rooted at `chupa/__main__.py`, recognizing both import idioms. The component is exercised directly through real Journal instances and injected clocks in disposable state. The later production activation fences and migrates this dormancy assertion.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_storm.py` names `test_storm_construction_and_reads_are_idle` (no construction reads/writes/clock calls, empty read/count, no files or signals from projection); `test_occurrence_signal_shape_and_identity` (exact envelope/key/body, producer digest unchanged, nullable stage/origin, invalid signature/id/body/key/ticket refusal with repair road, unrelated records ignored); `test_occurrence_replay_is_once` (same key/body no-op retaining timestamp, changed data refused, distinct arrivals sharing a signature or deduped message counted separately); `test_window_boundaries_and_signature_isolation` (exact one-hour constant, below/equal/above lower boundary, now included, future excluded, journal order, other signatures excluded); `test_occurrences_survive_roll_and_restart` (real journal rotation, active and rolled history, expired-key replay still no-op, reconstructed count, active torn-tail tolerance and strict corruption propagation); `test_occurrence_append_failures_and_crash_replay` (append failure gives no successful projection, before/after durable-append crash reconstruction and replay); `test_ledger_has_no_trip_side_effects` (more than five occurrences produces only occurrence signals, no Box output, notification, trip, or dispatch mutation); and `test_storm_ledger_is_dormant` (transitive import-closure scan calibrated to fail when either import idiom makes the module reachable). Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_storm.py tests/test_journal_roll.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_box.py`; unfenced suites are unchanged preservation suites. Use injected seams and disposable state, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits.

- **Owner:** Existing `chupa/storm.py` owns threshold evaluation, deterministic trip identity, and the trip signal, extending the occurrence ledger and producer wiring. `chupa/box.py` remains the sole queue-record writer and owns deduplicated report publication through its existing Message schema and filesystem seam. `chupa/daemon.py` composes this boundary with the lock holder's Journal, Box, and injected clock; `chupa/__main__.py` activates it for production producers in `run` and the bootstrap `drain`. Host inbox intake is not built by this row.

Replay of one observation reuses its index; a distinct observation takes a distinct position. Equal lines at different positions are distinct arrivals; rereading the same source snapshot reuses the same ids after partial failure. Preserve main-checkout resolution and the ingest return shapes. A journal-holding ingest uses that checkout's composed Box; retain callback-free standalone ingest behavior.

  Define `STORM_TRIP = "storm_breaker_trip"`. A trip is one `EventType.SIGNAL`, null envelope ticket, key `storm-trip/<trip_id>`, with body exactly `{kind: storm_breaker_trip, signature, trip_id, first_occurrence_id, crossing_occurrence_id, emitting_stage, emitting_origin, held}`. The occurrence ids name the first live occurrence and the threshold-crossing occurrence in journal order; stage and origin come unchanged from the crossing arrival, never from the deduplicated Box record. `trip_id` is the lowercase SHA-256 hex digest of the UTF-8 compact JSON array `[signature, first_occurrence_id, crossing_occurrence_id]` (separators `(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=True`). `held` is `{emitting_stage, emitting_origin}` for stages in the closed pipeline set `implement | check | review`, otherwise null.
