### 19.P3.storm-producer-wiring Dormant Box arrival-to-occurrence wiring

- **Owner:** Existing `chupa/box.py` owns the validated arrival callback at its single enqueue boundary, including signature-dedup hits; it remains the sole queue-record writer. `chupa/storm.py`, supplied by `storm-ledger`, owns occurrence validation, replay identity, and the journal-derived window. `chupa/daemon.py` owns an explicitly invoked composition hook binding that callback to the lock holder's existing Journal and injected clock. Add no module, producer loop, task, counter file, or production caller. The hook constructs a Box with the existing root and filesystem seam and the ledger callback; construction performs no reads, writes, or clock calls.
- **Records:** Add an optional keyword-only arrival callback to `Box` construction and an optional keyword-only `occurrence_id` to `Box.enqueue`. A Box without the callback preserves existing enqueue behavior and return shape `(message_id, created)`; existing direct constructors and enqueue callers remain valid. A Box with the callback requires a nonblank producer-supplied occurrence id before any durable operation. A producer owns that id: distinct arrivals receive distinct ids, and retry or crash replay of the same arrival reuses its id. Do not allocate it from queue sequence, signature, deduplicated message id, wall time, or callback invocation count. The later production activation supplies ids at production callers; this construction ticket does not change them.

  After validating the incoming Message and occurrence id, and reading valid existing queue records, invoke the callback exactly once per enqueue invocation, before either returning a signature-dedup hit or writing a new queue record. Pass keyword arguments exactly `{signature, occurrence_id, emitting_stage, emitting_origin}`: `signature` is the digest already computed by Box's existing signature recipe, `emitting_stage` is the arriving `stage` (nullable), and `emitting_origin` is the arriving `origin`. Use the incoming producer fields even when the stored message has a different diagnostic stage, origin, or summary. Preserve the existing paired stage/outcome validation, class-specific validation, normalized reason, and digest computation; no signature recipe changes. Invalid input or corrupt queue evidence invokes no callback and writes nothing. Callback/read/write failures propagate without a successful enqueue result. A callback failure precedes queue mutation; a queue-write failure after a successful callback is retried with the same occurrence id.

  The callback delegates to the predecessor occurrence writer, which durably appends `EventType.SIGNAL`, null envelope ticket, key `storm-occurrence/<signature>/<occurrence_id>`, body exactly `{kind: storm_occurrence, signature, occurrence_id, emitting_stage, emitting_origin}`. Journal is the sole durable event writer. Matching key/body replay is a no-op retaining the original timestamp, including after rotation, restart, or window expiry; conflicting data fails closed with the road to supply a fresh id for a distinct arrival or repair the producing evidence. Several callback invocations replaying one arrival yield one occurrence; several distinct arrivals deduplicating onto one Box record yield several occurrences. Preserve `STORM_WINDOW = timedelta(hours=1)` and the predecessor half-open window `(now - STORM_WINDOW, now]`, active/rolled reads, signature isolation, and corruption refusal. No additional signal, constant, Message field, event type, envelope field, ticket frontmatter, or authoritative arrival record is added.

  Preserve Box's current Message fields, sequence/id/filename allocation, initial pending status, null verdict/resolution, and dedup return of the original id with `created: false`. A dedup arrival records its occurrence without rewriting the stored message, even when that message is resolved; verdict and resolution stay unchanged. `messages`, `pending`, `get`, `record_verdict`, and `resolve` are not arrival producers and never invoke the callback.
- **Observable:** Direct invocation of the dormant daemon hook with a real Box and Journal records each valid arrival once, including dedup hits and non-pipeline producers, while Box still stores one record per signature. Before the occurrence append, a crash leaves no occurrence or queue write; after it, replay completes an interrupted enqueue without duplicating the occurrence. This row emits no trip, failure report, notification, dispatch hold, or release, at any count. Bootstrap consumer cadence, retry accounting, control behavior, and production producers remain unchanged.

  Dormancy is behavioral under `19.I`: the existing CLI/daemon production-composition harness never calls the new hook or arrival callback, and ordinary production Box arrivals emit no storm occurrences. Calibrate that assertion to fail when production invokes the hook. If importing the hook makes `chupa.storm` reachable, migrate only the predecessor `tests/test_storm.py` import-absence assertion to this behavioral proof in `tests/test_storm_producer.py`; preserve its occurrence, replay, window, rotation, failure, and no-trip obligations. Production reachability and caller-supplied arrival ids activate in `storm-notification-activation`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_storm_producer.py` names `test_new_and_dedup_arrivals_record_occurrences` (real dormant hook, exact callback/event shape and order, distinct ids sharing one signature, unchanged `(id, created)` and queue allocation); `test_arrival_replay_survives_restart_and_roll` (same id before/after restart, rotation, and expiry records once with the original timestamp, conflicting data refuses); `test_dedup_preserves_resolved_message_and_incoming_identity` (no queue rewrite, verdict/resolution retained, incoming nullable stage and producer origin retained, non-pipeline arrivals counted); `test_invalid_arrivals_and_callback_failures_do_not_publish` (missing/blank ids, invalid Message, corrupt queue, ledger refusal, and append failure yield no successful result or queue write); `test_enqueue_crash_replay_finishes_once` (failure before/after durable occurrence and Box publication, stable-id replay yields one occurrence and one message); `test_box_operations_without_arrivals_are_idle` (construction and non-enqueue operations never call the arrival seam, unbound Box keeps existing behavior); and `test_storm_producer_hook_is_dormant` (production composition never invokes the new seam, calibrated activation fails the proof, more than five direct arrivals cause only occurrence signals and no trip/report/notify/hold). `tests/test_storm.py` preserves the predecessor ledger obligations with only import dormancy migrated if necessary. Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_storm_producer.py tests/test_storm.py tests/test_box.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_journal_roll.py`; unfenced suites are unchanged preservation suites. Use injected clocks and disposable state, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits.

### 19.P3.checkpoint-push Dormant checkpoint push with restart-safe retry

- **Owner:** `chupa/checkpoint.py` owns the checkpoint trigger fold and asynchronous push Effect; `chupa/daemon.py` exposes an explicitly invoked dormant boundary using the lock holder's existing Journal, Effects, Timers, Git, Box, and injected Clock. Construction starts no task and performs no push or durable mutation. `chupa/git.py` owns a public async `Git.push(dir, remote, branch)` operation, returning no result on success and raising the existing `GitError` on nonzero exit. It runs exactly `["git", "-C", str(dir), "push", remote, branch]` through its existing process-exec seam, with the required child environment and timeout; reject empty or option-shaped remote/branch arguments before exec. No shell, force push, remote creation, provider-key inheritance, or direct process call. The shipped target is `origin`, branch `main`; an optional mirror is not required by this row. Continuous production invocation belongs to `serve-activation`.
- **Records:** Define engine constants `CHECKPOINT_MERGES = 5` and `CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL = timedelta(hours=24)` in `chupa/checkpoint.py`. Read ordered `Journal.read()` history across all segments. Count code admissions from `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION` records whose body has `to: merged` and a non-null `commit`; ticket-plane commits and run-lane `commit: null` records do not advance this count. The admission lane remains their sole writer; this row neither changes nor duplicates them.

  A window's number is the count of successful checkpoint Effect completions, initially zero. Its stable, attempt- and run-excluding Effect key is `checkpoint-push/<window>` (decimal window number), with null envelope ticket. Reuse `Effects.run`: its sole writer appends `effect_intent` with body `{}` before the external action, and `effect_completion` with body exactly `{result: {merged: <nonnegative integer>}}` only after `Git.push` succeeds. `merged` is the code-admission count observed immediately before that push, under the caller's serialized writer ownership. A completed window's recorded count is the next window's merge baseline; initially the baseline is zero. Neither stdout/stderr nor a failed return code is a successful result. A push exception must escape the Effect action before completion can append; catch it only outside `Effects.run`. An intent without completion remains retryable with the SAME key, including after restart. A completed key is never pushed again. A crash after the remote action but before its completion may repeat the push; do not claim remote exactly-once delivery.

  Use existing `Timers.arm`, `reconstruct`, and `fire_due`, whose sole durable writer preserves `timer_armed`/`timer_fired` bodies exactly `{timer_id: <string>, deadline: <aware-UTC isoformat string>}`, null ticket and null key. The current window's timer id is `checkpoint-push/<window>`; its deadline is first explicit invocation's injected now plus `CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL` for window zero, or the previous checkpoint completion's journal timestamp plus that interval thereafter. Reuse an already armed deadline on recovery, never restart-relative delay. A matching fired timer means daily passage, not push success. Ignore timers for completed windows, including an old timer firing after a merge-triggered success. The trigger is due when the current count minus the successful baseline is at least `CHECKPOINT_MERGES`, or the current window's daily deadline has passed (equality included). Both causes select ONE current window. Apart from the failure signal below, no new event type, reserved journal `checkpoint`, signal, frontmatter, configuration key, counter file, processed marker, or separate ledger is introduced.

  Failed-push arrival identity is owned by `chupa/checkpoint.py`, not invented by the seeding caller. For each actual push execution, `attempt` is the one-based count of `effect_intent` records for its exact `checkpoint-push/<window>` key, including the intent just appended by `Effects.run`, folded across all segments under the serialized writer lock. Every new execution, including a retry in the same window after restart, gets a new attempt number. A caught `GitError` or process-exec failure first records one `EventType.SIGNAL`, null envelope ticket, key `checkpoint-push-failed/<window>/<attempt>` (decimal integers), body exactly `{kind: checkpoint_push_failed, window: <nonnegative integer>, attempt: <positive integer>, error_code: <git_exit | process_failure>}`. `chupa/checkpoint.py` requests this append; Journal is its sole durable writer. `git_exit` means `GitError`; `process_failure` means an exception from the push's process-exec seam. Capture only failure of the push action, never an intent/completion append failure, cancellation, or journal corruption. This signal records a failed execution, never checkpoint completion; exception detail belongs in the redacted engine log, not the journal. A crash before the failure signal leaves only an incomplete push intent: retry it, never infer failure from intent alone.

  Enqueue that signal's soft `failure_report` through the supplied existing Box with `origin="checkpoint-push"`, `stage=None`, `outcome=None`, stable `reason="checkpoint push failed"`, and `occurrence_id=arrival_id("checkpoint-push", window, attempt)` using the existing `chupa.storm.arrival_id`. Its exact recipe is `storm-arrival/` plus the lowercase SHA-256 hex digest of the UTF-8 JSON array `["checkpoint-push", window, attempt]`, with integer components, separators `(",", ":")`, and `ensure_ascii=True`. The summary names `origin/main` and the recorded error code; configured-secret redaction applies to captured diagnostics. Box remains the sole Message writer and owns its existing signature deduplication and fields; use the supplied production `storm_producer` Box, never construct a callback-free replacement. Distinct failed executions receive distinct occurrence ids even when their reports signature-dedup to one Message; replay of one failure signal reuses its id and contributes no new storm occurrence. Do not derive identity from window alone, Box id, normalized reason, timestamp, randomness, or an in-memory counter.

  Every explicit poll, including recovery, replays the recorded failure signals in journal order through that same Box before attempting another push, including signals from completed windows. Replay introduces no additional push, failure signal, or processed marker; Box and the existing storm ledger retain their original report and occurrence identities across rotation, restart, and window expiry. Matching duplicate failure key/body is one failure; malformed shapes, conflicting bodies, or a key/attempt without its corresponding preceding push intent fail closed with the road to repair the producing evidence, never rewrite history. A crash after the failure append, after the storm callback append, or after the Box write replays the same failure identity; successful replay may then attempt a fresh push, whose failure would be a distinct arrival. Failure append or Box callback/read/write errors propagate, never fabricate completion or advance the window. The paved road is to repair the remote or credentials and allow the next checkpoint poll to retry. Failure leaves the window, merge baseline, and daily deadline incomplete; the next explicit poll, or restart recovery poll, retries while the trigger remains due. Journal corruption or a failed durable write is not swallowed as push success.
- **Observable:** Direct invocation under the existing writer lock pushes local main after five code merges or one injected day, including a quiet day; below both thresholds it performs no push. Success advances the window once; repeated calls and reconstruction cannot duplicate a completed window. Failed or interrupted pushes re-fire without another five merges or another day's wait. Push failure never rejects an admission, pauses dispatch, changes main, or blocks subsequent ticket processing. The shipped component remains dormant: ordinary CLI/drain composition never invokes its push boundary; importing it is permitted, and serve activation later supplies the recurring caller. Bootstrap drain admission, control, recovery, and merge-queue behavior remain unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_checkpoint.py` names `test_checkpoint_merge_and_daily_triggers` (zero/four/five admissions, ticket-plane and null-commit exclusion, exact daily equality, quiet day, simultaneous causes select one push); `test_checkpoint_success_advances_once` (exact intent/completion shapes, baseline and next deadline, repeat polls and restart skip completed keys); `test_checkpoint_failed_push_refires` (nonzero git exit and process failure produce a redacted, deduplicated soft Box finding, no completion, and same-key retry succeeds without fresh merges or time); `test_checkpoint_failure_occurrence_identity` (the real production `storm_producer` Box accepts the exact arrival recipe, repeated failed executions in one window and later windows have distinct ids despite one deduplicated Message, failure-signal replay adds no occurrence, null stage never holds dispatch, and intent ordinals survive restart/rotation); `test_checkpoint_failure_report_crash_replay` (exact failure signal shape and sole writer, crashes before failure append and after failure/callback/Box writes, replay after checkpoint success and window expiry, no extra push on report replay, redacted diagnostics kept out of the journal, durable-write/callback failures propagate without completion, and malformed/conflicting or intent-less failure evidence fails closed); `test_checkpoint_crash_windows` (before action, after action before completion, and after durable completion, reconstructed Effects/Timers recover correctly); `test_checkpoint_timer_survives_restart_and_roll` (original deadline and fired-but-uncompleted window remain due across restart and journal rotation, stale timers do not duplicate successful pushes); and `test_checkpoint_boundary_is_dormant` (production CLI composition never invokes the new boundary, calibrated to fail when it does, while direct boundary invocation exercises the real component). `tests/test_git.py` extends `test_op_argv` and the public-operation enumeration in `test_option_shaped_or_empty_refs_are_refused_before_exec` for push, preserving required environment/timeout, argv pinning, and GitError obligations. `tests/test_mergequeue.py` preserves admission and main-green behavior unchanged. Verification for the implementing seed runs `uv run pytest tests/test_checkpoint.py tests/test_git.py tests/test_mergequeue.py tests/test_effects.py tests/test_restart_timers.py tests/test_box.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py`; unfenced suites are unchanged preservation suites. Use injected clocks/processes and disposable repositories, never live remotes, real-model calls, or wall-clock waits.

### 19.P3.storm-dispatch-hold Live bootstrap storm dispatch hold and resume

- **Owner:** Existing `chupa/storm.py` owns the journal-derived live-trip and release projection and threshold re-arming. `chupa/control.py` remains the sole control-decision writer; `chupa/daemon.py` extends the existing `PauseConsumer` to expose storm identities through its ONE shared inbox and discovery projection. `chupa/drain.py` owns filtering held stems at dispatch selection, before any dispatch accounting, and polling control when only storm-held work remains. `chupa/__main__.py` wires the same storm projection and control consumer into the live bootstrap drain. Add no module, second inbox, journal writer, Box writer, worker task, or global dispatch pause. This row is the sole production activation of storm dispatch suppression; stage selection in the later continuous serve composition remains section 12's contract.
- **Records:** Reuse `STORM_THRESHOLD = 5`, `STORM_WINDOW = timedelta(hours=1)`, and `STORM_TRIP = "storm_breaker_trip"` from the predecessor. Occurrences remain null-ticket `EventType.SIGNAL` records keyed `storm-occurrence/<signature>/<occurrence_id>`, body exactly `{kind: storm_occurrence, signature, occurrence_id, emitting_stage, emitting_origin}`. Trips remain null-ticket signals keyed `storm-trip/<trip_id>`, body exactly `{kind: storm_breaker_trip, signature, trip_id, first_occurrence_id, crossing_occurrence_id, emitting_stage, emitting_origin, held}`. The ids identify the first live and crossing occurrences; `trip_id` is the lowercase SHA-256 digest of the UTF-8 compact JSON array `[signature, first_occurrence_id, crossing_occurrence_id]`, separators `(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=True`. `held` is `{emitting_stage, emitting_origin}` exactly for `implement | check | review`, otherwise null. Preserve diagnostic nullable strings, producer-owned occurrence identity, dedup-hit counting, the half-open `(now - STORM_WINDOW, now]` window, and the predecessor's occurrence-before-trip-before-report ordering and crash recovery. Journal is their sole durable writer.

  Fold active and rolled journal segments through `Journal.read()`. A valid unreleased trip with a closed pipeline stage holds a bootstrap ticket only when its stem equals the recorded `emitting_origin`; `emitting_stage` is diagnostic in this whole-ticket selector. Null or non-ticket origins and null/non-pipeline stages generate no dispatch hold. Validate trip shape, key, identity, and occurrence references before exposing a hold; malformed or conflicting evidence refuses with the road to repair the producing evidence, never silently discard a hold or rewrite history. Time-window expiry does not release a journaled trip. Construction is idle; live holds and releases are projections, never another held-state file or authoritative cache.

  Reuse `<state_dir>/control/inbox/<request_id>.json`, object exactly `{request_id: nonempty ASCII letters/digits/_/-, lifecycle_id: nonempty str, verb: pause | resume | kill, hold_id: str | null}`; filename matches request id, pause/kill require null hold id, and resume requires a nonempty hold id. A storm release targets the exact live trip id. The existing inbox alone appends null-ticket, null-key signals with body exactly `{kind: control_decision, request_id, lifecycle_id, verb, hold_id, decision: accepted | stale | rejected, reason}` BEFORE applying release. Its journal fold consumes each request once; accepted resume adds only its named identity to `ControlProjection.released_hold_ids`. Stale lifecycle, unknown/released hold, premature resume, and malformed requests release nothing and retain the existing paved refusal. A storm release emits no separate release signal, cap `keep`, terminal, or Box message.

  A recorded accepted resume after its matching trip is durable release evidence across restart; reconstruct it before exposing holds. The new lifecycle still refuses old inbox requests, and old accepted decisions never release a different trip. After release, re-arm that signature using only distinct occurrences later in journal order than its accepted release, intersected with the existing one-hour window. Old occurrences and arrival replay cannot immediately recreate the released hold; six new arrivals can create a fresh deterministic trip and its one predecessor-owned P0 failure report. While a trip is unreleased, additional occurrences do not create another trip/report. Releasing one signature or trip leaves all others held, including another signature holding the same stem.

  `<state_dir>/control/active.json` retains exactly `{lifecycle_id: str, hold_id: str | null}` while running and `null` on retirement; `PauseConsumer` alone writes it through the filesystem seam. Discovery selects the dispatch pause first, then an unreleased admission hold, then the earliest unreleased live storm hold in journal order. Inbox validation sees every live identity, not only the advertised one. Matching resume releases only that identity; publish the next remaining hold. Discovery is derived and recoverable from the journal, never release authority. A failed trip/decision append or projection publication propagates without claiming an applied mutation; reconstruction after a durable decision finishes its projection without another decision.
- **Observable:** The production bootstrap drain excludes storm-held stems from both fresh offers and retry re-offers BEFORE retry draws, machine Reject `keep`, running transitions, or dispatch-side work. Unrelated eligible tickets continue in the existing fresh-first priority/age order; existing dependency, premise, cap, and supersession rules remain intact. A trip does not interrupt an active offer. At the next boundary, refresh control and storm evidence before selection and re-read preparation after a control wait. No selection side effect is permitted for a held stem.

  When no unrelated offer is eligible but otherwise-dispatchable storm-held work remains, keep the lifecycle and writer lock alive, publish the hold identity, and poll the existing control inbox through injected Sleep. A matching live `resume` makes the same ticket eligible in the SAME drain invocation; kill still unwinds and stops, and the existing injected-clock `drain.max_runtime_hours` ceiling still bounds the wait. Neither a held stem nor its blocked dependents imply successful drain completion. With no dispatchable storm-held work, preserve ordinary quiescence. Resume does not reset lineage caps, edit tickets, triage Box records, clear a dispatch pause or admission hold, or manufacture a terminal. Bootstrap admission stays inline and holds-exempt; this is dispatch suppression. External notification remains Phase 4's transport, with escalation supplied by the predecessor's one trip and one report.

  At seed authoring, fence every predecessor storm test carrying a dispatch-absence assertion, especially `tests/test_storm_notification_activation.py::test_storm_activation_does_not_hold_dispatch_or_notify`, and migrate only that assertion to positive hold/release evidence, preserving its no-external-notify obligation. Apply `19.L` caller and contradicted-test closure to any changed composition signature; keep public signatures when a change is unnecessary. Do not migrate immutable seeding snapshots.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_storm_hold.py` names `test_live_drain_holds_only_emitting_stem` (real CLI/drain composition, six dedup arrivals, all three pipeline stages, unrelated work still dispatched in order); `test_storm_hold_precedes_all_dispatch_accounting` (fresh offer, retry and Reject re-offer, no cap draw/keep/running/dispatch for the held stem); `test_non_pipeline_and_non_ticket_trips_do_not_hold` (null/unknown stages, null/non-ticket origins, existing trip/report preserved); `test_live_resume_releases_in_same_drain` (only held work keeps its lifecycle alive, CLI publishes exact request, durable acceptance precedes dispatch, no cap reset); `test_storm_resume_is_identity_bound_and_once` (premature, stale lifecycle, wrong/old trip, duplicate scan, repeated request, later trip unaffected); `test_multiple_storm_holds_release_independently` (different stems and signatures, two holds on one stem, exact discovery ordering with dispatch and admission pauses); `test_storm_hold_survives_restart_roll_and_expiry` (all segments, unreleased hold persists beyond one hour, accepted release persists, old requests cannot affect a new lifecycle); `test_storm_release_rearms_on_new_arrivals_only` (old/replayed arrivals excluded, five new arrivals do not trip, sixth yields fresh identity and exactly one report); `test_storm_hold_crash_and_corrupt_evidence` (before/after trip, decision, and discovery writes, journal-before-mutation, recovered projection, paved corruption refusal); and `test_storm_wait_preserves_kill_budget_and_quiescence` (active offer finishes, unrelated work proceeds, held-only polling, kill, injected runtime ceiling, ordinary no-hold completion, Box never consumed). Use the real production graph with injected clocks/processes and disposable state, never real-model calls or wall-clock waits. Verification runs `uv run pytest tests/test_storm_hold.py tests/test_storm_notification_activation.py tests/test_storm.py tests/test_storm_producer.py tests/test_control.py tests/test_control_cli.py tests/test_daemon_pause.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_kill_cli_activation.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_box.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_journal_roll.py`; unfenced suites are unchanged preservation suites, with criteria-forced predecessor migrations fenced at authoring under `19.L`.

### 19.P3.serve-activation Continuous foreground production daemon

- **Owner:** The new `chupa/serve.py` owns the continuous foreground lifetime and recurring production consumers. `chupa/__main__.py` adds the argparse `serve` verb and invokes that lifetime using the same checkout and injectable seams as `run`/`drain`. Expose the production composition as an async in-process entrypoint, so tests and the later soak runner execute the graph the CLI uses. Reuse `build_daemon_core`, `DaemonAdmission`, `Scheduler`, `Watcher`, `DaemonTasks`, `TicketWriter`, the composed `MergeQueue`, `PauseConsumer`, `WorkerStop`, `WorkerFailureObserver`, `HeartbeatCycle`, `Restart`, `Timers`, the storm-producing Box, and the checkpoint boundary; their algorithms and record writers stay with their existing owners. `chupa/daemon.py` owns any necessary task/control binding. Keep existing composition callers valid without a second production path. This row starts one dispatch worker, never a concurrent worker pool. Daemon-mode candidate routing and retirement belong to `serve-merge-admission`; composing and polling the existing queue here does not replace the currently inline merge path.

  `chupa/stages.py` owns the next-stage and suspension seam in `run_stages`, reusing its existing Implement -> Check -> Review orchestration. `chupa/runner.py` retains settlement and failure custody; `TicketWriter` retains suspended continuations; `chupa/scheduler.py` owns selecting an eligible next stage; `DaemonAdmission` owns each executing callback through its unwind. The existing `Scheduler.dispatch_next` and `DaemonAdmission.dispatch` hold their slots through the whole ticket callback, and `run_stages` directly awaits all three stages: waiting inside that callback cannot free selection. Expose the boundary through these owners, never copy stage orchestration into serve or filter whole daemon tickets. Under `19.L` seam-owner, caller, and contradicted-test closure, the implementing seed adds `chupa/stages.py`, `chupa/scheduler.py`, `chupa/storm.py`, `tests/test_stages.py`, `tests/test_daemon_admission.py`, and `tests/test_daemon_config.py` to the registry fence floor and Context/on-demand partition, records the additions in its seeding test, and fences every additional direct caller or invalidated assertion found at the merged authoring head.
- **Records:** Read the existing resolved `Config.state_dir`, `worktree_root`, `scheduler.max_unmerged`, provider configuration, caps, and per-dispatch `ConfigSnapshot`. Ticket input remains `tickets/<stem>/ticket.md`, with the existing frontmatter, dependency, intake, supersession, quarantine, and journal-derived eligibility rules. Intake owns committing ticket-plane changes and its signals; Watcher owns its last-good cache and `watcher_parse_failure`; Scheduler owns priority/age ordering and single-flight selection. The lock-held dispatch wrapper records the existing `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION` body `{to: running}` on the selected ticket, null key, before invoking the prepared writer. The runner and admission lane retain sole ownership of run terminals, harvests, Effects, cap draws, and `to: merged`; serve never appends a duplicate terminal or treats a callback return as permission to fabricate one.

  Use one restart-unique lifecycle, one shared control inbox, one journal, and the startup coordinator's one `Timers` lifetime. Reuse `<state_dir>/control/inbox/<request_id>.json`, exactly `{request_id: str, lifecycle_id: str, verb: pause | resume | kill, hold_id: str | null}`, and the existing identity/type/filename validation. `ControlInbox` alone appends null-ticket/null-key `CONTROL_DECISION` signals with `{kind: control_decision, request_id, lifecycle_id, verb, hold_id, decision: accepted | stale | rejected, reason}` before mutation. Accepted pause/resume/kill fold into the existing projection; old lifecycles and mismatched holds mutate nothing. `PauseConsumer` alone publishes `<state_dir>/control/active.json` as `{lifecycle_id: str, hold_id: str | null}`, then `null` at retirement. Reuse `KILL_APPLIED = "kill_applied"`, null ticket/key, body `{kind: kill_applied, request_id: str, lifecycle_id: str}`: the existing kill-application owner writes it once per accepted request only after executor and worker cleanup finish. A signal stop is not a CLI request and fabricates neither a control decision nor a kill-applied record.

  Shared-journal custody includes the reused triage/Author path: its Driver and Effects must use `checkout.journal`. In merged code, `chupa/triage.py::triage_pass(checkout, llm)` internally constructs `Driver.from_config`, which creates a separate `Journal` and Effects owner; neither exposes a binding seam for the caller to supply `checkout.journal`. The `serve-activation` registry fence omits `chupa/triage.py` and `chupa/driver.py`, so satisfying this custody requirement requires an edit outside that earned fence. Correct the implementing seed's fence and caller closure under `19.L` before implementation; copying triage or replacing its writer through runtime monkeypatching violates the governing reuse and custody contract.

  Heartbeat remains empty bytes at `<state_dir>/heartbeat`, atomically written only by `Heartbeat.refresh` after `HeartbeatCycle`'s four responsive health probes pass; task existence alone is not health. Restart retains harvest -> `{to: abandoned}` -> worktree remove/prune; `Timers` retains `timer_armed`/`timer_fired` bodies `{timer_id: nonempty str, deadline: aware-UTC isoformat str}` and their supplied ticket/null key. Storm occurrence, trip, report, hold, release, and re-arm records remain exactly the predecessor storm units' records, with their existing sole writers and `implement | check | review` pausable set. The scheduler-side hold selection follows section 12; the bootstrap whole-ticket selector remains unchanged. Checkpoint retains `CHECKPOINT_MERGES = 5`, `CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL = timedelta(hours=24)`, `checkpoint-push/<window>` timer/Effect keys, completion `{result: {merged: nonnegative int}}`, and its soft Box failure report; invoke the predecessor rather than inventing push success or another retry ledger. Define only `SERVE_POLL_S = 0.1` seconds in `chupa/serve.py` for recurring idle polling through injected `Sleep`. No new journal event type, artifact, frontmatter, config knob, durable task registry, or service manager is introduced. Ownership references and responsiveness evidence are memory-only.

  Extend the existing storm projection to expose each unreleased trip's `held: {emitting_stage, emitting_origin}` to daemon selection; its bootstrap `holds()` stem projection keeps its existing contract. Match the candidate's stem AND next stage, never discard `emitting_stage`; null/non-ticket origins and null/non-pipeline stages suppress nothing. A suspended continuation is memory-only `{ticket: Ticket, ctx: StageContext, run: StagesRun, next_stage: implement | check | review}`. Keep its original `run.attempt`, per-run immutable snapshot, Driver/Effects, worktree, and completed results/artifacts; suspension has no `Outcome`, terminal, cap draw, or new durable record. `TicketWriter` alone retains and removes that continuation. It remains owned work for orphan-sweep exclusion even while both dispatch slots are free; it is neither a fresh ticket nor completed-but-unmerged work. The trip and accepted control decision remain the sole durable hold/release authority.
- **Observable:** Construction starts no tasks or external work. On entry acquire the existing single-writer lock and keep it until every owned task and executor has unwound. Under that lock, await the real core startup's orphan reconciliation, timer reconstruction/overdue firing, and storm recovery before intake, snapshot capture, pipeline preparation, or dispatch. Publish the current control identity before accepting offers. Recovery failure refuses startup without work or a ready claim. Supply the SAME control consumer to dispatch and merge composition, never another inbox or journal writer. Keep filesystem delivery, clock, waits, process execution, secret filtering, and captured diagnostics behind existing seams.

  Start and own concurrent watcher, serial merge-queue, and sequential Box consumers through `DaemonTasks`, plus the single dispatch worker and control/maintenance lifetime. Initial scanning supplies existing tickets; subsequent filesystem polling feeds changed/deleted stems to the real Watcher and preserves its debounce, last-good parsing, and removal behavior. Re-scan after admissions so newly lifted seeds and satisfied dependencies become selectable without restarting serve. Poll Box through the existing triage/Author path with its policy; never copy intake or authoring. Queue results remain consumer-owned, including typed conflict handoffs consumed by `TicketWriter` only after queue admission unwinds. An empty queue or empty Box is healthy idle work: continue waiting and discovering arrivals, never return drain quiescence. Serialize ticket-plane mutations with the existing writer/admission boundaries so background triage, intake, and maintenance cannot race an admission.

  Re-read control and storm evidence at every next-stage selection before that stage's calls, gates, cap draws, or Effects. A running stage finishes without storm preemption. If the next stage is held, return a nonterminal suspension through the runner and admission boundaries, retaining its producing run before fully unwinding the callback and releasing BOTH slots; do not invoke terminal handling or read an empty `StagesRun.last`. Keep at most one executing callback, with no waiting continuation coroutine or detached executor. Select unrelated eligible next stages in the existing priority/age order; a hold on Check or Review permits the same ticket's preceding unheld stages. A hold on initial Implement prevents fresh-offer accounting. Releasing the exact trip makes the retained next stage selectable in the same lifetime, after any active callback unwinds and current pause/backpressure checks pass. Resume consumes the prior stage's actual artifact under the original snapshot and run sequence, without another running transition, retry draw, worktree preparation, or replay of completed stages. Ordinary non-ok and already-satisfied short-circuits retain their existing settlement behavior. If only held stages remain, keep polling control without occupying either slot. `run`/`drain` drive the same stage orchestration to settlement under their existing whole-ticket selection contract.

  Live suspended continuations participate in the startup coordinator's owned-work predicate, so an idle sweep cannot reap their worktrees. Kill/signal/cancellation discards their memory-only selection entries only after executing callbacks and cleanup unwind, preserving their running evidence and worktrees for the next startup's existing harvest -> abandoned -> removal recovery. Restart does not resume a lost continuation from memory or manufacture success; its fresh run uses the next run sequence and still observes any unreleased storm trip.

  Control consumption continues during an in-flight stage and while pause or admission/storm holds block offers. Apply pause before the next offer's running transition, cap draw, snapshot, or dispatch-owned effect; active work is not preempted by pause. Matching resume releases only its identity; unrelated eligible work continues under storm holds. Each recurring maintenance cycle fires due timers, performs the idle-only orphan sweep, recovers storm publication, polls checkpoint push, and evaluates real worker/merge/Box/watcher responsiveness before heartbeat refresh. Do not sweep a live dispatch, cleanup, or admission. A quiet injected day still fires daily checkpoint work; a failed push stays soft and retries on the next due poll without stopping dispatch. A dead or wedged component stops heartbeat refresh, and Box-consumer death is exposed through that external heartbeat rather than asking the dead consumer to report itself. Notification transport remains Phase 4's row.

  Accepted current-lifecycle kill latches stopping before further offers, awaits the composed writer's real executor abort, then uses `WorkerStop` on the actual production worker tasks, cancelling and awaiting them only AFTER executor unwind. `WorkerFailureObserver` observes those same tasks and suppresses only the ordinary post-kill worker-failure callback; it never erases run-level failure custody, changes a terminal into success, or suppresses abort/cleanup failures. SIGINT/SIGTERM and outer cancellation stop admissions and use the same executor-before-worker cleanup ownership without forging inbox evidence. Normal ticket non-ok terminals stay runner-owned and do not terminate the daemon; an unexpected owned-consumer return, exception, or cancellation is a terminal daemon failure, observed and propagated after sibling cleanup, never automatically restarted. Return CLI exit 0 after an orderly kill/signal stop, 1 after terminal worker failure, and 2 for an engine-plane setup/lock/recovery refusal. Repeated kill and cancellation cannot detach cleanup or release the lock early. Preserve already-written terminals; otherwise leave interrupted running/effect evidence and its worktree restart-reconcilable. Retire control discovery and restore signal handling before releasing the lock. `run`/`drain` retain their bootstrap behavior and do not start these consumers. No daemon launch on host work is performed by the self-build; production-graph tests use disposable synthetic checkouts as section 18 permits.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_serve.py` names `test_serve_cli_uses_production_composition` (module CLI and async entrypoint build the same real graph, shared journal/control/Timers, construction idle); `test_serve_holds_lock_through_cleanup` (contention refusal, lifetime lock, discovery retirement before unlock, barrier-held and repeatedly cancelled cleanup); `test_serve_startup_precedes_all_work` (orphan harvest/reap and persisted timers before intake/snapshot/dispatch, recovery failures block work); `test_serve_waits_at_quiescence_and_discovers_work` (empty queue stays live, initial and later arrivals, edits/deletions, debounce/last-good behavior, merge rescan and successor dependency dispatch); and `test_serve_consumers_use_existing_writers` (single-flight production dispatch, sequential triage/Author whose real Driver and Effects share `checkout.journal`, actual queue results and post-unwind handoffs, serialized mutations, no duplicate running/terminal or fake merge).

  `test_serve_control_precedes_offer_accounting` covers pause/resume and stale lifecycle/hold requests, active work, held/idle polling, no post-kill accounting, and control during an in-flight production Driver call. `test_serve_kill_unwinds_executor_before_workers` uses a cancellation-resistant scripted LLM and actual owned workers to pin durable acceptance -> executor unwind -> worker cancellation/observation -> kill application -> discovery retirement -> unlock; disconnected or reordered wiring must fail. `test_serve_worker_failure_and_kill_suppression` covers ordinary failure, unexpected consumer return and cancellation, one ordinary callback without matching kill, suppressed shutdown callbacks after matching kill, observed exceptions, and abort/cleanup failures propagated without false application. `test_serve_signal_stop_and_restart_recovery` covers SIGINT/SIGTERM/outer cancellation, no invented requests/decisions, retained orphan worktree and unmatched intent, preserved terminal, and the next production startup's clean reconciliation; audit each producing journal with the existing invariant auditor.

  `test_serve_recurring_maintenance_uses_injected_time` advances at least 24 injected hours, exercises timer passage, idle-only sweeps, storm recovery, checkpoint daily/merge triggers and failed-push re-fire, and proves no wall-clock wait or live remote is needed. `test_serve_heartbeat_requires_responsive_components` independently wedges/fails each real core consumer and the main loop, proving refresh stops while healthy active and idle work refresh. `test_serve_storm_hold_and_resume` exercises the predecessor trip/report through real production selection, unrelated work, exact-identity release, and no duplicate storm writer. In `tests/test_daemon_composition.py`, add `test_production_serve_graph_is_reachable` and replace any serve-absence assertion with positive production evidence. Migrate only the serve-invalidated dormancy assertions in `tests/test_daemon_tasks.py`, `tests/test_kill_worker_stop.py`, `tests/test_kill_failure_suppression.py`, and `tests/test_heartbeat.py` to real serve activation while retaining the run/drain absence proofs and component invariants; preserve `tests/test_storm.py::test_storm_ledger_is_reachable` as positive production reachability evidence. Apply `19.L` activation/contradicted-test/caller closure at seed authoring to any additional predecessor test that the real wiring forces, including checkpoint dormancy, recording fence additions there; never migrate historical seeding snapshots.   `tests/test_serve.py::test_serve_storm_suspends_stage_and_frees_selection` exercises each pausable stage through the real scheduler/admission/runner/stage graph: held Check/Review retains preceding artifacts, both slots release, unrelated work proceeds, the same stage on another origin remains eligible, initial Implement is held before accounting, and no callbacks overlap. `test_serve_storm_resume_retains_producing_run` pins exact-trip release with multiple holds, unchanged attempt/snapshot/worktree/artifacts despite config edits and unrelated merges, no repeated completed stage or accounting, no terminal on suspension, and one terminal from its existing settlement owner; include ordinary non-ok and already-satisfied short-circuits. `test_serve_suspended_run_cleanup_and_restart` pins sweep exclusion while retained, held-only control polling, pause/kill/signal/cancellation ordering and repeatedly cancelled cleanup, preserved evidence, next-startup abandonment before dispatch, fresh-run identity, and an unreleased trip surviving restart; audit every producing journal. ### 19.P3.serve-merge-admission Production daemon candidate admission

- **Owner:** `chupa/merge.py` owns the explicit admission-mode boundary and the pre-offer code-lane and seed-safety checks; `chupa/serve.py` alone selects daemon admission in the production serve composition. `chupa/mergequeue.py` retains sole ownership of pending offers, serial processing, rebase, mechanical resolution, post-rebase safety, integration, admission holds, and checked-tree validation. Reuse that queue, never copy its admission algorithm into serve or inline merge. `chupa/runner.py` owns stage settlement and the call from `drive` to admission; `chupa/daemon.py` owns `TicketWriter` and post-unwind conflict-handoff consumption. Under `19.L` caller/seam-owner closure, the implementing seed adds `chupa/runner.py` and `chupa/daemon.py` to the registry fence floor and Context/on-demand partition, records those additions in its seeding test, and re-greps direct callers against the merged authoring head. No new module is introduced. Preserve the public bootstrap dispatch result contract and the existing no-mode inline `merge(ctx, ticket, *, attempt)` behavior; mode selection is an explicit in-memory composition input, never inferred from a lock, control consumer, event loop, journal contents, or config value.
- **Records:** Admission mode has the closed values `inline | daemon`, with `inline` the default and `daemon` requiring the serve-composed queue; an unknown mode or absent daemon queue refuses setup with a paved road to supply that queue at serve composition, never falls back to inline admission. A settled successful Review supplies the existing parsed `Ticket`, producing run's integer `attempt`, and pre-rebase branch head `reviewed_sha`. Read committed code-lane changed paths against main through `Git.diff_names`, and reuse `Candidate` with `{stem, reviewed_sha, changed_files, ticket_text, review_text: str | null, seeds: list[SeedOnMain], seed_checks_text: str | null}`. `gather_seeds` reads journal intake signals with `signal: ticket_intake` and `seeded_by: <offering stem>`; each `SeedOnMain` is `{stem, ticket_sha: str | null, ticket_text: str | null}` from main's committed blobs, and seeding Check's committed `Invoice.seeds` in `tickets/<stem>/checks.json` supplies approvals. `PostRebaseGate` refuses any committed `tickets/**` path; `SeedSafetyGate` requires each seed's committed blob to exist, pass ticket grammar, and have an exact-SHA `approve`. Both prechecks are hard for daemon admission and use the existing finding codes and paved roads. Dirty checkout copies are never seed custody. Prechecks write no terminal, run no Verification or model call, and mutate neither main nor the branch.

  Offer only the existing `(Ticket, attempt)` to the serve-composed `MergeQueue`, once for that producing run. Pending ownership and result delivery are memory-only; introduce no durable ready record, frontmatter, artifact registration, event type, config key, or new `StageResult.outcome`. Ordinary results retain `StageResult`: `ok` with `Admission` (existing artifact provenance plus `{stem, commit, reviewed_sha}`), or `gate_failed` with null artifact and existing findings/cost. `MERGE_SPEC_VERSION = 1` stays owned by merge. An unresolved conflict retains immutable `ConflictHandoff` `{stem, reviewed_sha, conflicted_paths: sorted unique list[str], findings: list[Finding], approval_invalidated: true}`, never a StageResult or a successful dispatch result.

  The queue retains its predecessor record writers and constants: `CONFLICT_FACTS = "merge_conflict_facts"`, `RED_STREAK = "merge_red_streak"`, `TREE_MISMATCH = "merge_tree_mismatch"`, `RED_STREAK_LIMIT = 3`, and the existing identity-bound hold/release protocol of `19.P3.admission-holds-activation`. Queue-owned null-key, original-stem `EventType.SIGNAL` bodies remain `{kind: merge_conflict_facts, conflicted_paths: sorted unique list[str], resolving_rung: none | mechanical | rework, strategy_paths: sorted unique list[str], integration_red_paths: sorted unique list[str]}`, `{kind: merge_red_streak, stems: list[str], limit: 3, hold_id: str}`, and `{kind: merge_tree_mismatch, checked_tree: str, main_tree: str, hold_id: str}`. Merge's `write_squash` alone runs Effect key `merge/<stem>/<attempt>`, completion `{result: {commit: <real main SHA>}}`, and appends one original-stem `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION`, null key, body `{to: merged, commit: <real main SHA>, reviewed_sha: <original pre-rebase SHA>}`. Preserve `squash_message` subject and `chupa-ticket`/`chupa-reviewed-sha` trailers. The runner alone owns non-ok harvests, failure dispatch, caps, and terminals; serve never stamps a second terminal or invents a commit. No GO-baseline or supervised-merge hold activates during the self-build; those remain Phase 6's cutover work.
- **Observable:** Construction performs no offer, processing, git mutation, Verification, or task launch. Only the real serve-selected path routes a Review-ok run through the daemon boundary. Other stage outcomes and `already_satisfied` never offer. Before any offer, judge the committed branch's code-lane and main's committed seed approvals; a refusal returns `gate_failed` with paved findings and leaves main and the candidate intact. Once prechecks pass, offer exactly once to the SAME queue polled by the production serve merge consumer, sharing its context, journal, and control consumer. Dispatch must not report `merged` merely because an offer was accepted, because `process` returned an empty list, or while its candidate remains pending behind a hold. Retain producing-ticket/attempt ownership until that candidate's own result is consumed. Waiting for admission must release any ticket-plane boundary the serial merge consumer needs; control polling and identity-bound resume remain live while admissions are held. Do not process admission concurrently in a dispatch callback or construct a second queue to bypass a hold.

  The queue restores lifted tracked ticket-plane deletions from the branch's committed head (`Git.restore(..., source=stem)`) before rebase, then runs its existing resolution ladder, safety tier, full integration tier, and checked-tree assertion. Preserve existing ticket Verification/base-red attribution and always-hard host-check behavior. Clean rebases carry the pinned approval without another model review; mechanical resolution still regates. Integration-red leaves main green and the branch/worktree present; safety refusal does likewise. Consume an unresolved handoff through `TicketWriter.consume_handoff` only AFTER `process` has unwound its active admission and serial slot, preserving the predecessor Rework/failure-terminal custody and invalidated approval. Never run Rework inline under admission or coerce a handoff into success. Admission itself does not retire a refused/handoff candidate; subsequent failure-spine/Rework disposition remains with its existing owner.

  Success uses the queue's real squash writer, asserts the admitted tree, retires via `Git.worktree_remove` (remove + prune) and `Git.branch_delete`, and exposes the one real Admission to dispatch; serve's rescan then observes the merged dependency and any lifted successors. A tree mismatch remains the existing post-mutation engine defect/hold, never a refusal claiming main stayed untouched; it preserves the branch for recovery. Cancellation and exceptions preserve the queue's abort-before-unwind behavior and serve's executor/task cleanup ownership, never fabricate a terminal or orphan an admission task. `run` and `drain` keep inline admission and never offer/process or wait on daemon admission holds. No live daemon-on-host launch is needed to prove activation.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_merge.py` adds `test_daemon_prechecks_precede_offer` (committed ticket-plane change, missing/malformed/wrong-SHA seed approval, missing/invalid seed blob, dirty-main copies, hard refusal despite soft configured severity, no offer/rebase/Verification/main mutation, retained candidate, paved findings). Preserve `test_bootstrap_pipeline_keeps_inline_admission` and `tests/test_mergequeue.py::test_inline_admission_does_not_use_merge_queue`, proving run/drain never offer or process even when the composed daemon queue has a hold. Add `test_admission_mode_is_explicit` for construction idleness, unchanged default inline behavior, refusal of an unknown mode or missing daemon queue, and no daemon selection from control/lock/journal presence.

  `tests/test_serve.py` adds `test_serve_routes_settled_run_to_composed_queue_once` (production async serve entrypoint with scripted Implement/Check/Review, real producing attempt, one offer on the actual consumer's queue, failed/non-ok stages never offer, no early merged return); `test_serve_admission_rebases_regates_and_retires` (moved main, lifted tracked deletions, both gate tiers and ticket Verification on the rebased candidate, real checked/main tree equality, original pinned SHA/trailers, one Effect/merged event, actual branch/worktree retirement and dependency rescan); `test_serve_admission_refusal_keeps_main_green` (precheck/safety refusal and individually green but combined integration-red branches, candidate preserved at refusal, findings consumed by the existing failure spine, next candidate proceeds below the red-streak limit); `test_serve_conflict_handoff_runs_after_admission_unwinds` (declared mechanical resolution still regates; unresolved conflict aborts, hands off with invalidated approval, and invokes real Rework only after slot release, no duplicate terminal); and `test_serve_admission_holds_and_cleanup` (three distinct integration-red stems, tree mismatch, exact lifecycle/hold resume, continued control polling, pending work never falsely merged, cancellation/exception cleanup and retained recovery evidence). Each test enters through real serve composition and settles production stages; direct harness calls to queue admission are not activation evidence. Audit each producing journal with the existing invariant auditor.

  Verification for the implementing seed runs `uv run pytest tests/test_merge.py tests/test_mergequeue.py tests/test_serve.py tests/test_daemon_composition.py tests/test_rework.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_control.py tests/test_audit.py`. Unfenced suites are unchanged preservation suites. Use disposable synthetic checkouts, scripted LLMs, injected clock/process/filesystem/sleep seams, and barriers; no real-model calls, remote mutation, or wall-clock soak. Apply `19.L` caller and contradicted-test closure to any additional files the merged serve predecessor makes necessary, record additions at seed authoring, and never migrate historical seeding snapshots.

### 19.P3.worker-recovery-disposition Run-scoped orphan recovery evidence

- **Owner:** `chupa/reconcile.py` owns the recovery disposition signal inside the existing `reconcile` reap loop, shared by scaffold on-entry reconciliation and `Restart` startup/idle sweeping. Reuse the supplied Journal, Git, configured worktree root, and orphan harvester; introduce no module, recovery implementation, caller signature change, or separate alert transport. `chupa/journal.py` retains ownership of `run_seq`, `TERMINAL_STATES`, the event envelope, durable append, and the closed `SIGNAL_NAMES` set. Under `19.L` seam-owner closure, the implementing seed adds `chupa/journal.py` to the registry fence floor and Context/on-demand partition and records that addition in its seeding test, for registration of the recovery signal name at its write seam.
- **Records:** Preserve `orphans` exactly: a ticket's `running` transition or unmatched effect intent since its last terminal makes it orphaned; every terminal closes that history, including a non-ok terminal with incomplete effects. For each reaped stem, capture its nonnegative integer `run_seq(journal.read(), stem)` BEFORE appending its abandonment, using the same sequence for harvest and recovery evidence. Prior terminal count identifies the producing run, never the incremented sequence belonging to its next entry. Preserve harvest of an existing worktree, the null-key `{signal: harvest_failed, error: str}` signal on harvest error, and the null-key `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION` body exactly `{to: abandoned}`.

  `reconcile.py` alone owns `RECOVERY_ALERT = "recovery_alert"`; `journal.py` adds the literal `"recovery_alert"` to `SIGNAL_NAMES` with this consumer, preserving the append seam's refusal of unlisted names and its requirement for exactly one of `signal` or `kind`. Reconciliation appends one `EventType.SIGNAL` per successfully reaped orphan, envelope `ticket: <orphan stem>`, `key: null`, existing signal schema version, body exactly `{kind: recovery_alert, disposition: alert, outcome: abandoned, reason: "orphaned run", run_seq: <captured integer>}`. The fixed reason describes the orphan fold, not exception text or diagnostic output. Append/fsync the abandonment first, then this signal, then remove the worktree through `Git.worktree_remove` and preserve the existing prune behavior. A missing worktree still receives both records. No new event type, artifact, frontmatter, configuration key, counter, Effect, or signal idempotency key is needed: the abandonment terminal prevents that run from being reaped or alerted again.
- **Observable:** The addition is active wherever the existing reconciliation runs; it changes evidence only. Preserve harvest -> abandoned -> recovery alert -> remove/prune ordering, including harvest failure and missing-worktree cases. A repeated restart/sweep of closed history writes no additional alert or terminal; a later genuinely open run of the same stem receives its own alert with the next producing sequence. Clean or empty journals remain no-ops. The signal sends no Box mail or notification, draws no cap, invokes no diagnosis, changes no routing or eligibility, fabricates no effect completion, and does not prevent a reaped stem's ordinary fresh-worktree re-run.

  Preserve lock-held startup and idle-sweep ownership boundaries; never reap live work. Journal or Git failures propagate. An alert append failure prevents worktree removal and successful recovery return; the already-durable abandonment remains terminal history. A cleanup failure after the alert leaves that evidence intact. A restart after either failure does not backfill an alert or re-reap that closed run; existing fresh-entry teardown handles leftovers. No crash replay subsystem is added between the two durable appends.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_reconcile.py` adds `test_recovery_alert_records_producing_run` (exact constant, envelope/body, running and intent-only orphans, multiple stems, prior terminals, pre-abandonment sequence, unchanged abandonment); `test_recovery_alert_precedes_worktree_removal` (harvest -> terminal -> durable signal -> remove/prune, missing worktree, preserved harvest-error signal); `test_recovery_alert_is_once_per_reaped_run` (repeat reconciliation and a new Journal handle emit nothing for closed history, later open run of the same stem alerts with the incremented sequence); and `test_recovery_alert_failures_preserve_terminal_history` (alert-append failure prevents cleanup, cleanup failure preserves alert, restart after each writes nothing further, retained worktree and propagated error). `test_recovery_alert_does_not_change_disposition` proves the reap returns the same stems, emits no Box/notification/cap/diagnosis/routing records, leaves historical non-ok runs untouched, and permits ordinary re-entry. Preserve the existing clean/empty-journal, intent-only, missing-worktree, idempotence, and re-run tests. Audit running-witness recovery journals with the existing invariant auditor; intent-only fixtures preserve the auditor's existing missing-running-witness finding rather than inventing a transition to make them green.

  `test_recovery_alert_records_producing_run` also asserts `RECOVERY_ALERT` membership in `SIGNAL_NAMES` and round-trips the exact recovery envelope/body through the real `Journal.append`/`read` seam. Preserve `tests/test_vocabularies.py::test_every_engine_signal_name_is_listed`, `test_listed_signal_names_are_accepted`, and `test_unknown_signal_name_is_refused_at_append` unchanged, proving the registered name has its consumer and the write vocabulary remains closed.

  Under `19.L` contradicted-test closure, the implementing seed adds `tests/test_restart_timers.py` to its fence and Context/on-demand partition and records that addition in its seeding test: `test_restart_reuses_orphan_reconciliation` currently asserts abandonment is the last record at removal. Migrate that assertion to the ordered abandonment plus exact recovery signal, retaining its shared-writer, startup/sweep, live-ownership, and failure assertions. Re-grep against the merged authoring head for any additional criteria-forced predecessor assertion. Verification for the implementing seed runs `uv run pytest tests/test_reconcile.py tests/test_restart_timers.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_journal.py tests/test_vocabularies.py tests/test_audit.py`; unfenced suites remain unchanged preservation suites. Use disposable repositories, injected seams, and deterministic failure barriers; no real-model calls or wall-clock soak.

### 19.P3.outbox-only-admission Schema-validated no-code run admission

- **Owner:** `chupa/stages.py` owns report discovery, schema validation, stage evidence, initial Verification, and the existing `lift_outbox` ticket-plane writer. `chupa/merge.py` owns the shared admission writer used by inline merge and the daemon queue, the `Admission` model, the merged terminal, and retirement. Extend these owners in place; introduce no module or second lift/admission path. `chupa/mergequeue.py` retains its existing rebase, gate tiers, holds, and checked-tree assertion, calling the same admission writer without a signature change. Report schemas and their `KNOWN_ARTIFACTS` registrations remain the emitting machinery's responsibility; this row registers no report of its own.
- **Records:** Read the committed branch diff through `Git.diff_names`, the producing stem and integer `attempt`, Implement's existing `ok | already_satisfied` claim, and `KNOWN_ARTIFACTS`, the mapping from report basename to its closed Pydantic schema. Qualifying report paths are repo-relative strings under exactly `tickets/<stem>/` whose basename is registered, other than `run.md`; validate the actual bytes against that registration. `ticket.md`, run records, checks, reviews, unknown files, another stem's reports, and malformed registered reports never qualify. An inherited canonical report alone is not this run's output.

  At Check, retain the existing purge of registered OUTBOX reports before the ticket's Verification commands, so Implement-written or inherited reports cannot substitute for Verification-produced evidence. Gather the qualifying paths after those commands and require that they are among the files the existing checks lift will actually write. Preserve its byte-equal canonical-copy exclusion and validation-before-commit behavior. Check succeeds only after that lift completes. The lift remains the sole report committer, with Effect key `ticket-plane/<stem>/<attempt>/checks` and completion body `{result: {kind: checks, paths: list[str], commit: <ticket-plane SHA>}}`. At merge regate, read this producing run's exact completed lift and its named report's committed main blob, validating it against the current registration; never infer custody from an arbitrary checkout file, an old run's completion, or `checks.json.passed` alone. Regate still executes the ticket's Verification and validates any registered reports it produces; it neither registers new artifacts nor creates another report-lift lane.

  Preserve `Invoice`, the existing gate codes, Verification command results/base-red attribution, and the pinned Review approval. Extend `Admission.commit` in place to `NonBlank | null`: a code admission retains its real squash SHA, while a run-lane admission returns `commit: null`, the original `reviewed_sha`, and a nonblank `produced_at_sha` equal to main's current SHA after the report lift. Preserve `MERGE_SPEC_VERSION = 1`. The shared merge Effect keeps key `merge/<stem>/<attempt>` and returns `{commit: null}` for the run lane without squash or Git commit. Its sole merged-terminal writer appends one null-key `EventType.STATE_TRANSITION`, envelope `ticket: <stem>`, body exactly `{to: merged, commit: null, reviewed_sha: <original reviewed SHA>}` after successful gates and durable report custody. Do not use the report's ticket-plane SHA as a code commit or fabricate squash trailers. No new event, signal, frontmatter field, configuration key, outcome, or durable admission receipt is introduced.
- **Observable:** Activate the `19.L` run-lane exception at initial Verification and merge-time regating: an `ok` run with an empty committed diff passes the empty-diff rule exactly when it has qualifying schema-valid report output in the current checks lift. At merge that output must already have reached main through the matching lift. Ordinary empty `ok` work still fails with a paved road to commit code, produce a registered report through Verification and leave it uncommitted for the lift, or claim `already_satisfied` when all criteria already hold. Preserve green empty `already_satisfied` settlement before Review; a report producer claiming `ok` proceeds through Review and admission.

  The exception relaxes only the empty-diff rule. Preserve scope, run-record, diff-budget, ticket-schema, approval, seed-safety, host-check, rebase, integration, and hold behavior. Refuse an attempted run lane containing uncommitted code outside its OUTBOX; unknown OUTBOX debris does not establish eligibility. Any committed `tickets/**` path still fails code-lane safety with the existing road to leave OUTBOX files uncommitted. A nonempty code diff continues through the ordinary squash path even if it also emits a report. A failed command, missing qualifying report, invalid schema, or failed lift cannot produce a successful run-lane admission. Refusals leave the branch/worktree for the existing failure spine and write no merged terminal.

  Both inline admission and the serve-selected queue use the shared writer's empty-diff behavior. A successful run lane leaves the rebased code tree unchanged, preserves the queue's checked-tree assertion, and retires its worktree and branch once through `Git.worktree_remove` (remove + prune) and `Git.branch_delete`. Same-run Effect replay creates no second report commit or merged terminal. Existing journal eligibility observes `to: merged` even with a null commit; the existing auditor accepts that explicit null, and checkpoint code-merge counting excludes it. Preserve their current contracts without modifying their owners.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_stages.py` adds `test_outbox_only_check_accepts_registered_report` (empty committed diff, `ok` claim, real Verification-produced registered report, schema validation, checks-only lift, and progression to Review); `test_outbox_only_check_requires_current_report` (no output, only run/check/review files, unknown output, foreign-stem output, malformed report, Implement-written/stale/inherited output, byte-equal skipped lift, and uncommitted code cannot qualify); and `test_outbox_only_check_failure_never_admits` (red Verification, missing named report, validation failure, and lift failure remain failures with paved findings). Preserve `test_empty_diff_claimed_ok_fails_verification`, `test_already_satisfied_is_proven_by_green_verification_and_skips_review`, and the existing report purge, invalid-report, and checks-commit custody tests.

  `tests/test_merge.py` adds `test_outbox_only_admission_records_null_commit_and_retires` for inline and daemon admission through the real shared writer: current-run lifted report, pinned approval, rebased Verification, one merge Effect with null commit, exact terminal and artifact provenance, unchanged code tree, no squash/code commit/trailers, actual retirement, and same-run replay without duplication. `test_outbox_only_merge_regate_requires_lift_custody` rejects absent, wrong-run, wrong-stem, unknown, missing committed, or schema-invalid lift evidence and red regate while retaining the candidate and writing no merged terminal. `test_outbox_only_exception_preserves_code_lane_safety` proves committed ticket-plane files and dirty code are refused, ordinary code plus report still squashes, and stale/missing approval still refuses. Audit successful producing journals and prove their null-commit terminals satisfy dependency eligibility without advancing checkpoint code-merge counts. Reuse production stages, real lift/Git/Effects seams, disposable repositories, scripted agents, and injected failures; no hand-committed report is success evidence.

  Verification for the implementing seed runs `uv run pytest tests/test_stages.py tests/test_merge.py tests/test_mergequeue.py tests/test_serve.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_drain.py tests/test_audit.py tests/test_checkpoint.py`. Unfenced suites remain unchanged preservation suites. Apply `19.L` caller and contradicted-test closure at seed authoring if the merged head exposes additional forced files; record additions in the seeding test, never migrate historical seeding snapshots.

### 19.P4 Phase 4 -- Reliability and providers

- *Deliverables:* the stuck/spiral watchdog as a staged chain -- the in-flight adapter EVENT STREAM (per-tool-call JSONL events surfaced at the driver/provider boundary to a consumer seam; spool capture, cleanup, and the post-terminal harvest reader unchanged); the NOTIFY transport (section 15 `Notifications.notify(argv)` seam running the config-declared argv command as an Effect with section 6's key domains and conservative re-send reconcile -- the ONE transport section 13 names; its merge IS the production flip for Phase 3's escalation emitters, storm breaker and red streak); the spend-without-progress DETECTOR + hard-timeout wiring (token/cost accumulated from the event stream since the last OBSERVED mutation to a `## Scope fence` path across section 9's three regions -- a tool-call request is never a mutation; a stream reporting no usage charges `limits.est_cost_per_call_usd` at call start; trips at spend exceeding 3x the serving row's per-call basis, an engine constant; ONE run-seq-keyed notification, the stuck alert run-BLIND; process-group kill on stuck); the ACTIVATION flipping EVERY production driver call, review included, to the watched path. Provider cooldown Timers and failover over the CONFIGURED candidates (`providers.py` owns ordered selection and served-identity stamps; `timers.py` + the journal own cooldown state; section 6's one-payload-per-session rule). The reliability battery and its run.
- *Exit reads (committed artifacts only):* every member green in `tickets/reliability-run/reliability-battery-report.json` (schema `ReliabilityBatteryReport` in `chupa/artifacts.py`), member order CLOSED: `classified_quota_exhaustion` (quota classified, cooldown deadline persisted), `all_candidates_cooling_recovery` (ordered candidate exhaustion, the all-cooling hold, the matching `timer_fired` recovery, the recovered served identity), `unclassified_failure_preservation` (an unknown failure stays `unclassified`), each run over the battery's multi-candidate FIXTURE registry and green only when observed equals expected and the member-local journal passes the auditor (emitters: machinery `reliability-battery`, producer `reliability-run`). The spiral soft-band notification (fired once, no auto-kill, its notify intent/completion pair in the run journal) and the hard-timeout group kill are obligations of `watchdog-detector`/`watchdog-activation` and their merged production-composition tests -- the hard-timeout test asserting the killed run is harvested -- read from `watchdog-activation`'s committed passing `checks.json`.
